* intent

beef, 10-04, verbatim, in order:

/"shell signal will change now and our trajectory vector will get a few more handlers and it will get freaky. i think at one point we will start “holding” the execution or elevate it into a chromed session. going into an interactive session. we will also need to iterate some signature matching methods and probably signature encoding methods, maybe even vector traversal, lets see."/

/"we start with something else tho. in m76 we moved terminal to lighthouse and m7x we vectorized the runtime. the runtime is now a pure function. we can boot a instance within the shell context. all we need is to define the right instance config."/

/"we need authentication storage for ghost. i dont want to re login to my shell after f. e. signing up from the cli, logging in etc. auth credentials must live somewhere. on anima its localstorage. what would be ghost? two approaches: linuxy/vivay(ledger)."/ → /"we add a new env var viva-credentials-file where we either default to vivay or swt linuxy if wanted. both possible. we can store many credentials! many lighthouses."/

/"this brings another capability into relief that m76 enables. we should be capable of pretty simply create a new localhost lighthouse that just has one user, and automatically signs up everyone to anything. lighthouse/localhost."/

/"the shell signal. ghost will get a new capability. i write in shell viva yap yap yap yap. i want my default/home mode to receive that. signal must be constructed to match either different vectors or different signatures in one. the old ones are commands. instance/use etc. commands. we will also add references. like i type viva home. or viva flashcards. that should match the modes behind the variable reference home, or by the slug of whatever mode in my daemon has slug flashcard. and then open the whatever that represents. like a application buffer or a harness or whatever. well in ghost now only harness. if reference is ambivalent, we fall into a selection screen. aka we filter down through the vector trajectory applicative results until only one signature matches. this requires now tooling on matching and traversal. commands. references."/

/"and then we have the no match where we assume a default mode and just start chatting directly with home. harness. some default mode home gets that unmatchable yap as turn. well. we get a choice screen. send to $home, retry search, open as turn."/

/"and then we go into the full ghost app. which requires us as logged in AND capable of starting runtime and lighthouse aka full instance in the same process as the ghost execution. should be fine, we already do that elsewhere. still. deserves to be done right. like, controllers, stdin/out, etc. and then it requires that we have multiple render styles in ghost and i THINK we already had a quest on this or a compact or we have something in the code maybe? where we go from plain cli to active continuous client."/

/"home is an identity trait."/ · /"command and reference. can they live on the trajectory? its just matching and parsing after all. study signature and signal. matching. deeply."/ · /"this is big. probably 3 quests when it all comes together."/

10-05, on the render ladder, after the four-grip table and the alt-screen correction:

/"we have. execution where we run and done. in the execution we can elevate. … interactive component - ctx head stays inside the effect. chrome - where we render our UI but stay in the effect. and shell where we drop the ctx / head back from the effect and expect it to continue to run."/ → /"chrome and render are realy the same thing, only different screen covering mechanism? or rather, inline vs fullscreen?"/ → /"we might want to be able to render different chrome in the effects no? repo provides, but other components/modes can supply their own. so. its really view.render(data, Component) where component is either fullscreen or inline. i dont see view emit as relevant. so we have two ways. rendering inside the effect - might be fullscreen or inline. rendering a persistant app - like chat harness whatever."/ → /"render interactive and shell. i guess chrome might be how we name the custom interactive fullscreen components."/

/"i want to give buffers controllers. we want stdin/out etc on buffers. same controller as elsewhere. buffer hooks stay on buffer. is that buffer the same as what we render into a frame on anima?"/ · /"we already HAVE a buffer concept. modes emit them, daemons hold them, anima renders them into a frame. we wrap a terminal around them. a terminal (entity in lighthouse and container in anima) exists to render buffers and docks. thats what we will do on ghost as well. … on ghost, do we only ever render one terminal? no tmux capabilities built in? … only ever one buffer per cli. hmmm. yes. for sure we can add a table somewhere to open and navigate and crud multiple terminals. trivy. we use the same ways and methods that anima uses. ok. … inline and interactive. interactive rendering the vivalence chrome and multiple terminals."/

/"we dont start with rendering mode buffers tho. that comes later. i want to start with chat and the harness. that should be a straightforward first target. when i $viva home yap yap yap yap yap. then i want the effect triggering interactive, full shell chrome, runtime started if instance not already live - started meaning either in this process or on system level, depending on the config in the ledger.instance.runtime - , logged in with credentials from VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE, lighthouse connected, terminals and daemons loaded, mode harness connected."/

/"everything this connects to is already live, there is already an example of how to do it in the anima client, most mechanisms have implementations on demo level, and all the components we need for glue (controllers, signatures, vectors) are already in use. … this is a design challenge where you need to copy right."/

/"also investigate the runtime boot! you will have to know how paladin works too. ghost will need to instantiate runtimes in its execution. maybe in the trajectory vector itself? maybe trajectory resolves on interactive and we drop into a surrounding structure? that would make the trajectory a possible terminal spawner. hmm. might make sense to re-mount the trajectory inside the interactive cli."/

/"anima/src/typology/prototypes/conversation.js is very very old. i think we replaced the concept entirely."/ — confirmed: exported from =prototypes/index.js:3=, imported by nothing (the three svelte hits are the word =conversational=, the trait gate). It dies in this quest's residue.

* terrain — measured 10-04 · 10-05

Every claim is a file read or a command run this session; the two long traces (10-05) were run by two opus agents at medium and spot-checked.

** the signature family — nine subclasses, one base

| class | file | coerces from | nature of a node |
|---|---|---|---|
| =Signature= | =subsystems/typology/prototypes/signature.js= | string · array · object · fn | a tree node: =nature= · =trace= (parent) · =gauges= (children) |
| =Signal= | =prototypes/signal.js= | a STRING (shell-quoted) | path segments; flags on the LAST segment |
| =Pattern= | =prototypes/pattern.js= | string · ={nature, …valence}= | typed segment: =wildcard · remainder · parameter · literal= |
| =Action= | =prototypes/action.js= | string | exact-nature match |
| =ToolCall= | =prototypes/toolcall.js= | =a_b_c= | underscore path → =.signal= |
| =Path= · =Url= · =Span= | =prototypes/{path,url,span}.js= | fs path · url · — | — |
| =ShellSignal= | =systems/ghost/prototypes/shellsignal.js= | an ARGV ARRAY | segments; params + flags on segment ZERO |

** pattern types are a table — a kind is a row

#+BEGIN_SRC js
// subsystems/typology/prototypes/pattern.js:69
const patternmap = [
  ["wildcard",  (signature) => signature === "*",         (signal) => object.assign(signal, { type: "wildcard" })],
  ["remainder", (signature) => signature === "(.*)",      (signal) => object.assign(signal, { type: "remainder" })],
  ["parameter", (signature) => signature.startsWith(":"), (signal, pattern) => object.assign(signal, { parameter, parameters: { [parameter]: signal.nature } })],
  ["literal",   (signature) => true,                      (signal, pattern) => (signal.nature === pattern.nature ? signal : null)],
];
#+END_SRC

A =Vector= picks its signature class — =new Vector(ancestor, signature = Pattern)= · =withSignature()= (=prototypes/vector.js:4,13=).

** matching — what the dispatcher computes and throws away

| function | file:line | computes | keeps |
|---|---|---|---|
| =scope= | =gestalten/steer/match.js:19-27= | EVERY edge whose pattern applies | all |
| =feed= | =match.js:3-7= | first match with an effect, else =matches[0]= | ONE |
| =traverse= | =steer/dispatch.js:13-58= | the walk; =remainder= eats segments, numbers them =parameters[0..n]= (=:34-45=) | =[effect, carry, steps, position]= |
| =shotgun= | =dispatch.js:68-91= | every applicable leaf, each executed | all |
| =walk(vector, more)= | =dispatch.js:95-114= | interactive descent; asks =more(position.patterns)= when the signal runs short; throws =Short= · =Long= | — |

Precedence today is INSERTION ORDER (=scope= iterates the trie Map, =feed= takes the first effect-bearing match) — =codemap/typology.md= states it as a trap: /"NO segment-type precedence — declare literals before =:params="/. Standalone proof of the alternative — longest-prefix match with a default route, POSIX =--=, Alexa vocative, ACL envelope — stands at =systems/ghost/tests/address.test.js= (=14 passed | 0 failed=, 10-05).

Read whole 10-05 (the tangle's ground): every =patternmap= filter MUTATES the signal node it matches — =object.assign(signal, { type: "remainder" })= (=pattern.js:67,72,79=, =belt/object.js:244= assigns into the target) and the literal filter returns the node itself. =scope= tries EVERY edge on one node, so a root remainder stamps =type: "remainder"= onto a segment a literal then wins, and =traverse= (=dispatch.js:35=) reads =match.type= off that stamped node and eats the tail. Today's trees never put a remainder beside literals at one node; the yap edge does. Two readers of the stamp: =dispatch.js:35= and =shape/object.js:29=; =fromm.match(steps).parameters= (=fromm/index.js:49=) reads =parameter= · =parameters= off the winners (=http.js:16= · =socket.js:135,169= · =strategy.js:35,54= · =shard/batch.js:33=).

** the two tokenizers

=Signal= (=signal.js:5-67=): shell-quoted string → tokens; =--k v= · =--k=v= · =-k= · =-abc= · =--= terminator; flags on the LAST segment; =json.flags= reads =(this.fin ?? this).flags= (=:78=). =ShellSignal= (=shellsignal.js:5-41=): argv array; flags and params on segment ZERO. Ghost reads =ctx.signal.flags/params= on the ROOT. Two grammars for one shell.

** the trajectory and the view today

- Ghost's root: five middlewares — span → catch → =ctx.call= → cwd mount → render (=systems/ghost/mod.js:24-131=); the cwd middleware (=:76-84=) mounts an instance found at the shell cwd BEFORE dispatch; =ctx.interactive= ruled once (=:90=).
- sheets (=subsystems/sheets/mod.jsx:65-200=): =BufferControl= = five hook lists (=mount · render · tick · release · destroy=), =release(opts)= is the suspension's resolve; =scroll.emit= (one frame, =setTimeout(unmount, 0)=) · =scroll.render= (inline, resolves on release) · =buffer.render= (= =scroll.render= wrapped in =<Chrome>=, =:126-140=) · =buffer.shell()= (ink root mounted empty, =mount(child)= swaps a slot, =untilExit()= on esc/return) · =hijack(shell)= (reroutes =scroll.*= into the slot). =Chrome= = =<Background><Logo/>{children}</Background>= (=components/Chrome.jsx:6=) — a padded box. NO alternate-screen escape anywhere: =grep -rn "1049|altScreen|alternate|smcup" subsystems/sheets= → 0. The comment "alt-screen" at =mod.jsx:125= is false.
- callers: =pick= (=belt/pick.js:30=) · ledger =Init= (=trajectories/ledger/index.js:45=) · delete =Confirm= (=instance/delete.js:51-59=, the one =BufferControl= constructed by hand) · instance =Init= (=instance/init.js:149=) use =scroll.render=; =scroll.emit= ×4 (=help= =trajectories/index.js:163= · ledger =Doctor= =:160= · registry =Doctor= =registry/index.js:97= · =Effect= =mod.js:119=); =--buffer= is the only =shell()= caller (=mod.js:98=); nothing calls =buffer.render=. Components reach the control as the =buffer= prop and end with =buffer.release(opts)= (=ledger/Init.jsx:15,26,49= · =instance/Init.jsx:54= · =Search.jsx:28= · =Confirm= via =onSubmit=).
- =instance/run= holds the process by =await die.perpetuate()= (=run.js:24=) — a CHILD die; =Init.jsx:38= taps children while the wizard holds.

10. =ledger/die.js= — =boot(specs)= spawns CHILDREN (=process.js:24-48=, =clearEnv: true=), lock at =<ledger>/locks/<slug>.lock= with a token (=:48=), =integrate= waits for =Status:RUNNING= (=process.js:50-69=), =release= only on matching token. The runtime itself never touches the lock (hazard 18). The lock's API is THREE verbs — =Lock.write(record) · read() · remove()= (=prototypes/ledger/lock.js=; =read= pings the pid with =SIGURG= and clears a dead lock) — there is no =claim=; the record =Die.claim= writes is ={ pid, token, instance, status: BOOTING|ALIVE, processes: [{ process, pid }], started }= (=die.js:25-35=), =instance/stop= SIGTERMs =lock.pid= (=trajectories/instance/stop.js:15=), =instances/list= prints =processes[].process= as =running=.
12. ALREADY THERE (the 10-05 design asked for both): =shard.transmitter.inline(serve)= at =T/gestalten/shard/transmitter.js:218-248= — builds a =Request= from the ctx (method · headers · JSON body), calls the handler, reads JSON / event-stream (raw =ReadableStream=) / text / bytes back, =setError()= on =!ok=; the daemon (=R/lifecycle/daemon/integration.js:4=) and the test rigs (=tests/scenarios/{lighthouse,daemon}.js=) ride it today. What it lacks: the =Request= carries no =signal= (=fetcher= passes =request.signal=, =:261=), so a client abort never reaches a server generator. And =@vivalence/runtime= (=systems/runtime/typology.js=) already exports =Runtime= flat and =lifecycle.runtime.execution= as a namespace.
11. Handles after RUNNING: =die.controller= · =die.runtime.aperture= · =die.runtime.processes.{daemon,service}= (=find() · findOne({slug})=; NO =runtime.daemons[]=) · daemon entity: =aperture · call · lighthouse · cortex · entities{em, thread, turn, user, mode, …} · modes[type][slug] · services= · =mode.harness = shape.object(harness, echo)= — in-process, =echo= builds a bare ctx: no request, no =ctx.user= (only =thread.user=), no RequestContext (=shard.datamap.inject= not on this path), no =authorize=.

Hazards for an in-process host (the ones that bind this design; the trace lists 25): paladin is ONE singleton with ONE =instance= slot and a live =home= getter — one instance per process (4–8); =mount= memo never evicts (9); =publish()= mutates =Deno.env= (11); =registry.supply()= resets the pensieve on every boot (14); module-level Vectors are mutated per boot — a second boot stacks a second =bind= (15); the port is =VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE= — the same a spawned runtime binds (16); sqlite and migrations on disk are shared (17); no lock without =ledger.boot= (18); mikro context names fixed per slug (19); =Lighthouse= status subscription never disposed, beacons fire before =serve= (20); =verify= is HTTP even in-process (21); readiness via =control.controlled(die.controller)= (22); every harness turn arms =paladin.skills.{fs,shell}= with the host's permissions (25).

** baselines (pasted, one-shot)

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
deno test -A --no-check systems/ghost/tests/{shellsignal,pick,use,target}.test.js   → ok | 48 passed | 0 failed (1s)
deno test -A --no-check subsystems/typology/tests/{signal,signature,vector}.test.js → ok | 3 passed (23 steps) | 0 failed (592ms)

* the matching study — beef's question answered

/"command and reference. can they live on the trajectory? its just matching and parsing after all."/ — Yes. Both, and the yap, without a new pattern KIND; the standalone at =systems/ghost/tests/address.test.js= proves the algebra.

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
viva instance/use italian        command    literal · literal, param       — today's trie, unchanged
viva flashcards                  reference  a LITERAL opened at mount time — one edge per mode slug the instance declares, plus `home`
viva home what did i get wrong   reference + payload: `home` literal, the remainder is the TURN
viva flash                       reference  NotFound → pick over census(trajectory) with `flash` as the query → one row → re-dispatch
viva what did i get wrong        yap        the root `(.*)` remainder eats every segment → the default route = home
viva                             bare       help today; the shell once the chat cut lands
#+END_EXAMPLE

- a reference is a literal because the set is KNOWN before dispatch: =paladin.instance.daemons[].kernel= names the modes; the cwd middleware mounts before =traverse=; =home= is =identity.trait.home= read from the credentials row.
- the yap is the remainder that exists (=pattern.js:74=, =dispatch.js:34-45=). Formal basis: IP longest-prefix match with a default route · POSIX guideline 10 (=--= ends options, operands opaque) · router splat · FIPA-ACL envelope (receiver · performative · content) · Alexa's =ask <name> to <utterance>=.
- ambiguity is =scope=, rendered — =pick= over the candidates. Two laws owed in typology, both laws not kinds: precedence =literal < parameter < wildcard < remainder=; an =Ambiguous= failure beside =NotFound · Short · Long= carrying the scope.
- one grammar: =ShellSignal='s argv coercion becomes a coercion ROW on typology =Signal=; flags on the fin by =Signal='s law; =ShellSignal= dies.
- =walk(vector, more)= (=dispatch.js:95=) is the shape of the shell: a dispatcher that asks for the next signal — the trajectory re-mounted INSIDE the interactive as its stdin, /"the trajectory a possible terminal spawner"/.

* the architecture — copy anima, swap the transport and the surface

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
                 ┌──────────────── ghost process ────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
viva home <yap>  │ trajectory ──traverse──▶ /home (.*) effect                                        │
                 │     │                      │ elevates: view.capture(faces)                        │
                 │     │                      ▼                                                      │
                 │     │   session ── host(instance) ──▶ runtime.statics.launch                      │
                 │     │                │   inline  : die = run.js's die, execute(execution, die)     │
                 │     │                │            transport = inline(shape.http(die.runtime.aperture))
                 │     │                │   embedded: lock ALIVE? join — transport = fetcher + multiplex
                 │     │                │            else the detached supervisor as instance/start does   │
                 │     │                ▼                                                            │
                 │     │   client = anima's structures, unchanged:                                   │
                 │     │     Lighthouse(connection) ─ credentials row → $authority · $identity       │
                 │     │     Dataspace[Daemon, Terminal] ─ handshake EVERY recipe daemon (user row)     │
                 │     │     terminal = terminals.active ?? create()  ·  mode = home  ·  thread      │
                 │     │     soma.scan(mode.harness.dialogue.stream({thread, id, parts}))            │
                 │     │                ▼                                                            │
                 │     └──── re-mounted as the shell's stdin: walk(trajectory, more = input line)    │
                 │          a command renders inline in the chrome · a reference reseats the terminal│
                 │          a yap is a turn on the seated thread · esc → teardown                    │
                 └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
#+END_EXAMPLE

The one decision that makes it copy-right: the client ALWAYS talks to the runtime through a =Connection=, and the RUNTIME composes the paths — a client never does. beef, 10-05: /"the clients dont compose the paths. runtime does. and the runtime only offers calling on daemon level. so we can only reach the modes through daemon when they are part of a daemon. which they are to be harnessed. we might run runtimes inline and use a local connector. but we still go through the daemon to get to modes."/ So =mode.harness= as an object (=harnessed.js:225=, =shape.object(…, echo)=) is a SERVER handle, not a client path; the client reaches a mode only as =/daemon/<slug>/mode/<type>/<slug>/…= on the daemon's aperture, with the daemon's =authenticate= · =authorize= · user bind · RequestContext on the way (runtime trace 23.8–9). Two modalities for the runtime, his words: **inline** — the runtime runs in the ghost process, the connector is local (=shard.transmitter.inline(shape.http(die.runtime.aperture))=, the daemon's own trick at =R/lifecycle/daemon/integration.js:3-6=); **embedded** — the runtime is launched as a separate process that survives the ghost (=instance/start='s detached supervisor, =trajectories/instance/start.js:12-35=), and ghost joins it over fetcher + multiplex like anima. Auth, scopes and the enrollment handshake stay exactly anima's. The lighthouse is a service INSIDE the same aperture (=/attached/process/service/lighthouse/<slug>/…=), so one transport reaches both.

The second decision: anima's client structures are PURE by dogma (=anima.md=: /"structures (src/typology) are pure"/) and live in =systems/anima/src/typology/{stores,entities,prototypes}=. Ghost cannot import them (anima's =exports= are empty; the barrel is a vite alias). They move to a shared home (F9) — the stores =lighthouse · terminals=, the entities =daemon · terminal · mode(+traits) · thread · turn · buffer · intent · activity=, =Dataspace= — anima re-imports them from there. The ONLY anima-specific residue in them is =localStorage= (=lighthouse.js:186=) and the =Connection= built inside =daemon/dossier.js:58= — both already flagged as dogma violations in =anima.md=; this quest fixes them by handing =hydrate= a store and the dossier a transport.

* the law

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
One trajectory. A command, a reference and a yap are three edges of one trie, matched by one traverse; the shell re-mounts the same trajectory as its stdin.
A reference is a literal opened at mount, never a pattern that consults a store. Precedence is literal < parameter < wildcard < remainder, by law; ambiguity is the scope, rendered.
One signal grammar: typology Signal coerces the string AND the argv; flags live on the fin segment.
Three ways ghost holds the terminal, beef 10-06: cut (inline, one-off — a command runs and is done) · captured (inline, ongoing — interactive, the head stays held) · chrome (fullscreen, captured, ongoing). The alternate screen is one escape pair in one sheets primitive, never a word in a comment.
The client never calls the runtime by object; it speaks through a Connection. Hosting changes the transport, never the client; the runtime composes every path, a client composes none.
One instance per ghost process. A ghost that hosts a runtime claims the instance lock like instance/run; an instance ALIVE is joined, never booted twice.
Authority is a ledger record keyed by lighthouse origin — anima's key law — at VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE, default <ledger>/credentials.json; many lighthouses, one file.
The shell is a Terminal: a lighthouse row seated on a thread, rendering a dock first and buffers later — anima's verbs, no third way.
A handler sets ctx.effect; the shell renders. Nothing in a trajectory prints.
(proposed by the architecture pass, F15) A Vector is the one contribution surface: a mode's doors reach the trajectory as the strip the runtime published, rebuilt as edges under the mode's literal; the client still composes no path.
(proposed by the architecture pass, F16) In the dock bare text is a turn and a sigil opens the trajectory; in the shell argv commands win and -- ends them.
#+END_EXAMPLE

* milestones — the burndown

Each =blast → test → land → test → blast=; each boots green at its boundary. Sorted as beef suspects the burn goes — /"i suspect we will be closer to grammar-term-chat"/ — grammar first (pure typology + ghost, no runtime in the loop), term second (the render primitives are the floor every later surface stands on, so they land BEFORE the references that render a choice), chat last (it stands on both). Two re-sorts since 10-05, both for coupling: render ways (j) moved ahead of references (m) because =Choice.jsx= is written against the new =view.render=; the host (d) moved ahead of credentials (a·b) because =instance/lighthouse= reaches its lighthouse through =host()= and would otherwise be touched twice. The three groups remain the three quests (F1). Until the chat lands, a reference or a yap in a pipe resolves to its Resolution (the =address.test.js= shape) as the effect.

* the design — the tangle

** the comp — =Ghost Shell.dc.html=, 10-06
beef: /"i wrote a half assed incomplete design thingy. problem is that the design env doesnt really to tty very well. so i need to move here. apply the design solutions to our cut captured adn chrome. injest them into the trajectory vector. rework the tangle."/

The commission is =Ghost Shell.dc.html= (claude.ai/design project =877d7a04…=): a 120-col cell grid driven by =ghost-engine.js= (902 lines, fetched whole 10-06, kept at the session scratchpad =design/ghost-engine.js=). Siblings =Ghost Chrome (design).dc.html= and =Ghost Baseline (LIVE).dc.html= are not ported. beef's sketch over the earlier draft (=uploads/draw-743d7aae….png=) strikes the legend and proposals rail, so the rail holds no pane. Its proposals are read as data below.

| face | screen | lasts | top → bottom |
|---|---|---|---|
| cut | inline | one-off | =~ $ viva …= · effect · blank |
| cut held | inline | until close | =~ $ viva …= · picker / confirm / wizard · gone on close |
| captured | inline | ongoing | scrollback turns · slot · live turn · error · status row · input · hints |
| chrome | fullscreen | ongoing | crown · spine │ stage ─ transcript · input · hints |
| chrome < 100 cols | fullscreen | ongoing | crown · tabs · stage ─ transcript · input · hints |
| boot | either | until RUNNING | step rows · log tail · sign-in · =› booting…= |

The panes, each saying what it shows and where it comes from:
- *the effect* (cut): the verb's =ctx.effect= through =Effect=; doctors lead with a one-line verdict (=✕ 1 problem · KEY is REQUIRED and blank at daemon/hello=), then the tree. Source: the handler. Signals: =!= warn · =REQUIRED= bad · =—= dim · =ALIVE= ok.
- *the held component* (cut held): =Search= · =Confirm= · =Init=, inline under the command line. It holds the input (no caret) and is gone when it closes. Source: =ctx.view.cut.hold=.
- *the status row* (captured, above the input; inverse in chrome as the crown): =✦ <instance> · <identity> · <daemon> › [<type>/]<mode> [· <origin> · <launch> · ⚎ <i>/<n>] [· <terminal label>]=, with the one live state at the right edge (=● streaming= · =✕ error= · =verified= from 120 cols). The =✦= mark is coloured by standing (ok verified · accent connecting · bad unreachable · dim signed out). At 80 cols origin, launch and the type segment drop, then the label clips. Source: =paladin.instance= · =held.lighthouse.identity= · =session.seated= · =terminal.$buffer= · =thread.$buffers=.
- *the turns*: =you= in accent bold; the mode's slug after the brand mark =✦=, never "assistant"; one tool per glyph — =◐ name= running, =▸ name= done, =✕ name= failed. Source: =thread.$turns= · =session.$live= · =session.$echo=.
- *the slot*: whatever the last typed command painted, above the live turn; a new turn clears it. Source: =capture.cut=.
- *the input*: =› draft ▌= in accent, dim and caretless while a component holds the slot. Hints under it: captured =⏎ send · esc leave · ctrl-c leave · ctrl-f chrome=; streaming =ctrl-c stop · esc leave=; chrome =⏎ send · esc captured · ctrl-t terminal · ctrl-o mode · ctrl-f=.
- *the spine* (chrome, 24 cols, left of a =│=): a guided form of three steps, terminal → mode → thread. A done step collapses to =◇ label= plus its value; the open step is =◆=. The terminal step lists terminals (=●= active, =○= other, a second dim line =daemon › slug · turns=) and =+ terminal=; the mode step is a filtered list grouped by daemon (=n of N · ⌫ widen=); the thread step shows state, turns since, and =buffer #i/n · ⚎ phase=. Its hint is pinned to the bottom. Source: =held.terminals.$entities= · =held.daemons[].modes= · =thread=.
- *the tabs* (chrome under 100 cols, in place of the spine): =● label  ○ label  += with =daemon › mode= on the right. An open spine step takes the whole body.
- *the stage* (chrome, after the first turn): =<mode> · buffer #i of n · ACTIVE=, =set: k=v · …=, then a =─= rule over the transcript. The comp draws a mode's own view inside (oracle's corrections box, a flashcard). A TUI view kind does not exist, so the stage shows the buffer's =data= until one does.
- *the boot* (the elevation from verb to session): step rows =● instance · lighthouse · daemons · terminal · harness= with =◐ ○ ✕ != glyphs, the runtime's log tail while hosting inline, and a sign-in form in place when no credentials answer. It collapses to one dim line in scrollback.
- *the leave line*: =✦ left <mode> · <n> turns · <instance> still running | stopped=.

The comp's palette, verbatim, until beef orders the theme. Ghost writes the 16 ANSI names (=sheets/theme.js=); the hexes are the comp's ANSI preview and its truecolor reading of dapper zone 0:

| key | cut held | captured | chrome | spine step |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⏎ | pick | send | send | pick · seat |
| esc | abort | leave | → captured | back |
| ctrl-c | abort | stop · again leave | stop · again leave | — |
| ctrl-f | — | → chrome | → captured | — |
| ctrl-t | — | — | terminal step | — |

| n · x | — | — | — | new · close |

What the terminal answers differently from the comp — measured 10-06 in =ink@5.2.1/build=:
- *F12 never reaches =useInput=*. =parse-keypress.js:30= names =[24~= =f12=; =use-input.js:68= blanks the input of every non-alphanumeric key, and the key object carries no =f12= flag. =ctrl-f= (=\x06=) is the toggle.
- *ctrl-m IS ⏎*. =parse-keypress.js:145= reads =\r= as =return= before the ctrl-letter branch at =:178=, so the comp's mode step is unreachable. =ctrl-o= (=\x0f=) takes it, QUESTIONED.
- *the chrome cannot be =stdout.rows= tall*. ink repaints the whole screen per frame when the output height reaches the terminal's (=codemap/design.md=), so the chrome is =rows − 1= tall.
- *chrome → captured re-prints the thread*. The alternate screen keeps nothing, so leaving it remounts =Captured=, whose =Static= (keyed by thread) prints the thread's turns whole into scrollback. That is the comp's =setFace= flush, for free.

What the design wants and the system lacks — marked, not stubbed (pass 2: the sign-in and the verb-boots-the-session moved OUT of this list into the tangle, =*** pass 2=):
- a TUI view kind for a buffer (the stage's oracle box, the flashcard) — M13's stage shows =data= until it exists (doc open 1);
- the daemons' pips =● ●= on the status row and the =unreachable= standing — the client's per-daemon health atom is unread in ghost;
- a terminal label — no =LABELED= trait on the terminal row yet; =terminal/rename= writes one the way =persistDock= writes =DOCKED=, QUESTIONED;
- =thread/clear= — scrollback cannot be unprinted; not ingested;
- ctrl-b buffer cycling and the stage folded to its title at 80 columns (doc open 3);
- bare =viva= opens the session in the comp — today it is =help= (F20).


Paths in the =:tangle= headers are relative to =.ikiro/quests/=. A NEW file tangles whole; an edit to an existing file is a =diff= hunk with the container-rooted path on its first line, anchored to the lines read 10-05. Every snippet was written against the live tree read this turn — the quest cites the line it changes.

import { Box, Effect, React, Text, theme } from "@vivalence/sheets";

export function Doctor({ data }) {
  const problems = data.problems ?? [];
  const [first] = problems;
  return (
    <Box flexDirection="column">
      {problems.length ? (
        <Text>
          <Text color={theme.bad}>✕ </Text>
          <Text bold>{problems.length} problem{problems.length === 1 ? "" : "s"}</Text>
          <Text color={theme.dim}> · </Text>
          <Text color={theme.accent}>{first.key}</Text>
          <Text color={theme.dim}> is </Text>
          <Text color={theme.bad}>{first.verdict}</Text>
          <Text color={theme.dim}> at {first.at}</Text>
        </Text>
      ) : <Text color={theme.ok}>✓ no problems</Text>}
      <Box marginTop={1}><Effect data={data} /></Box>
    </Box>
  );
}
#+END_SRC

=Form.jsx= imports =Text= from ink and =theme= from =../theme.js= in the same hunk (its head imports =Box= alone today).

What the terminal still answers differently (pass 2): =F12= and =ctrl-m= as the doc writes them stay =ctrl-f= and =ctrl-o= (measured above); the doc's own keys table is the comp's. The palette's truecolor column maps to dapper roles — =default= text.ink · =bold= text.header · =dim= text.muted · =accent= signal.primary ink · =brand= signal.primary FILL (/"brand.outline was too faint on parchment"/) · =ok/warn/bad= signal.positive/caution/negative ink · =param= text.light — recorded for the token pass; ghost still writes the ANSI names.

** M1 — one grammar
The argv row is =ShellSignal='s grammar moved onto typology =Signal=, byte-for-byte in what it accepts but one token: the path is the first token that does not open with =--= (split on =/=) — =ShellSignal= made a segment named =--json= of =viva --json=; the row reads =viva --json instance/use x= as =viva instance/use x --json=. Every other token is a positional param unless it opens with =--=; =--k=v= carries a value, =--k= is =true=; nothing else is a flag. A bare =--= ends the flags: every token after it is a param, dashes and all (POSIX guideline 10 — the string row had it at =signal.js:23-26=, the argv row did not /(pass 3)/), and =argv= spells the terminator back when a param opens with =--=. Where the string grammar hangs flags on the LAST segment, the argv row hangs params AND flags there; the root answers for its fin through getters, so =ctx.signal.params= and =ctx.signal.flags= read unchanged at all 34 ghost sites. The setters pin an own property so the string coercion's =this.flags = flags= (=signal.js:64=) and =Object.assign(this, segment)= (=signature.js:17=) keep working. =rebase= is overridden because the inherited one re-coerces =this.absolute= — an array of strings, which the new row would read as argv. =Signal.tokens(line)= exposes the string tokenizer's quote-aware split WITHOUT its =--k=v= split — that split is the string row's, and handed to the argv row it reads =--slug= as =true= and =bar= as a param — so a typed line becomes the argv the shell itself would pass: one grammar. No caller in the tree builds a =Signal= from an array today (grep 10-05, widened at the fold to every =new Signal(= with a non-literal argument: =toolcall.js:27= · =batch.js:13= · =http.js:12= · =trie.js:6= · =object.js:3,24= pass strings), so the row collides with nothing.

#+BEGIN_SRC diff
// subsystems/typology/prototypes/signal.js:4
 export class Signal extends Signature {
   static coercions = [
+    [
+      (argv) => is.array(argv) && argv.every(is.string),
+      function (argv) {
+        const terminator = argv.indexOf("--");
+        const options = terminator < 0 ? argv : argv.slice(0, terminator);
+        const operands = terminator < 0 ? [] : argv.slice(terminator + 1);
+        const at = options.findIndex((token) => !token.startsWith("--"));

The 21 ShellSignal cases, folded into 20 against typology's class, plus the POSIX terminator case /(pass 3: 21)/ — =absolute= became =argv= (the old =absolute= answered argv; typology's answers natures), =params= on an empty argv is =[]= not =undefined=, and a flags-only argv has a =null= nature instead of a path named =--json=:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/signal.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assert, assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import { is, Signal } from "@vivalence/typology";

Deno.test("Signal(argv): coerces argv array into signature", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["instance/clone", "@from", "./to", "--flag"]);
  assert(is.signature(signal));
  assert(is.Signal(signal));
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): splits command path into nature chain", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["instance/clone"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, "instance");
  assertEquals(signal.fin.nature, "clone");
  assertEquals(signal.array.length, 2);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): single-segment command has no fin", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["install"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, "install");
  assertEquals(signal.fin, null);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): indexes positional params by position, read from the root", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["instance/clone", "@from", "./to"]);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["@from", "./to"]);
  assertEquals(signal.fin.params, ["@from", "./to"]);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): boolean flag", () => {
  assertEquals(new Signal(["cmd", "--flag"]).flags.flag, true);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): --key=value form", () => {
  assertEquals(new Signal(["cmd", "--name=viva"]).flags.name, "viva");
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): argv spells the original back", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["instance/clone", "@from", "./to", "--flag"]);
  assertEquals(signal.argv, ["instance/clone", "@from", "./to", "--flag"]);
  assertEquals(signal.absolute, ["instance", "clone"]);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): rebuild preserves chain, params and flags", () => {
  const original = new Signal(["instance/clone", "@from", "./to", "--flag", "--name=viva"]);
  const rebuilt = new Signal(original.argv);
  assertEquals(rebuilt.nature, "instance");
  assertEquals(rebuilt.fin.nature, "clone");
  assertEquals(rebuilt.params, ["@from", "./to"]);
  assertEquals(rebuilt.flags, { flag: true, name: "viva" });
  assertEquals(new Signal(rebuilt.argv).argv, original.argv);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): install instance localhost ~/test", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["install", "instance", "localhost", "~/test"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, "install");
  assertEquals(signal.fin, null);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["instance", "localhost", "~/test"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, {});
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): instance/stop with --force", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["instance/stop", "target", "--force"]);
  assertEquals(signal.fin.nature, "stop");
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["target"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags.force, true);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): bare command, no args", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["status"]);
  assertEquals(signal.params, []);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, {});
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): deep path paladin/scope/registry/mount", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["paladin/scope/registry/mount", "/path/to/instance"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, "paladin");
  assertEquals(signal.array.length, 4);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["/path/to/instance"]);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): multiple flags, mixed value and boolean", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["run", "target", "--mode=fast", "--detached", "--port=3000"]);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["target"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, { mode: "fast", detached: true, port: "3000" });
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): leading slash command path", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["/instance/run", "target"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, "instance");
  assertEquals(signal.fin.nature, "run");
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): empty argv", () => {
  const signal = new Signal([]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, undefined);
  assertEquals(signal.params, []);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, {});
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): flags only — no path, the flags on the root", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["--json"]);
  assertEquals(signal.nature, null);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, { json: true });
});

Deno.test("Signal: the string grammar is untouched and shares the getters", () => {
  const spoken = new Signal("instance/use --ledger");
  assertEquals(spoken.flags, { ledger: true });
  assertEquals(spoken.params, []);
  assertEquals(spoken.json.flags, { ledger: true });
  assertEquals(new Signal("users/123/profile").argv, ["users/123/profile"]);
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): a leading --flag is a flag, never the path", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["--json", "instance/use", "italian"]);
  assertEquals(signal.absolute, ["instance", "use"]);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["italian"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, { json: true });
});

Deno.test("Signal.tokens: a typed line becomes argv — quotes kept whole, --k=v kept one token", () => {
  assertEquals(Signal.tokens('flashcards quiz "me now" --fast'), ["flashcards", "quiz", "me now", "--fast"]);
  assertEquals(Signal.tokens("instance/create foo --slug=bar"), ["instance/create", "foo", "--slug=bar"]);
  const signal = new Signal(Signal.tokens("instance/create foo --slug=bar"));
  assertEquals(signal.absolute, ["instance", "create"]);
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["foo"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, { slug: "bar" });
});

Deno.test("Signal(argv): POSIX -- ends the flags; what follows is params, dashes and all, and argv spells it back", () => {
  const signal = new Signal(["home", "--json", "--", "instance", "use", "--fast"]);
  assertEquals(signal.absolute, ["home"]);
  assertEquals(signal.flags, { json: true });
  assertEquals(signal.params, ["instance", "use", "--fast"]);
  assertEquals(signal.argv, ["home", "--json", "--", "instance", "use", "--fast"]);
  assertEquals(new Signal(signal.argv).params, ["instance", "use", "--fast"]);
  assertEquals(new Signal(["--"]).params, []);
  assertEquals(new Signal(["--"]).flags, {});
  assertEquals(new Signal(Signal.tokens("home -- instance use")).params, ["instance", "use"]);
});

Deno.test("Signal.rebase: re-roots by path, never by argv", () => {
  const rebased = new Signal("a/b").rebase("root");
  assertEquals(rebased.root.nature, "root");
  assertEquals(rebased.absolute, ["root", "a", "b"]);
});
#+END_SRC

** M2 — two matching laws
Two laws, one cause. The stamp (terrain: every filter writes into the shared signal node) is what makes a root remainder beside literals unsafe, so =Pattern.apply= returns a record per match (F13) — then =feed= can rank by type because each record carries its own, and =traverse='s own stamps (=parameter · parameters= for the remainder, =dispatch.js:36-37=) land on the record, never on =ctx.signal=. The two readers of =match.type= (=dispatch.js:35= · =shape/object.js:29=) read the WINNER's record and need no change. =fromm.match(steps)= reads winners only. The =codemap/typology.md= trap /"NO segment-type precedence — declare literals before =:params="/ retires with this milestone (M14 re-stamps it).

A spread of a Signal node carries its own enumerable fields — =nature · trace · gauges= and, on a fin, =params · flags= (own since M1) — which is everything a step is read for (=nature= by =route()=, =input/output= by =guarded=, =parameter(s)= by =fromm.match=). The dead commented copy of the class (=pattern.js:88-152=) is deleted in the same hunk /(pass: the first draft's hunk left it standing while this sentence said otherwise)/ — its own =patternmap= once answered a parameter as ={ ...signal, parameter, parameters }=, the record form F13 asks for.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/typology/gestalten/steer/match.js
import { Ambiguous, NotFound } from "@vivalence/typology";

const RANK = { literal: 0, parameter: 1, wildcard: 2, remainder: 3 };
const rank = ([match]) => RANK[match.type] ?? 0;

export function feed(matches, signal) {
  if (!matches.length) throw new NotFound(signal);
  const ranked = [...matches].sort((one, other) => rank(one) - rank(other));
  if (signal.heir) return ranked[0];
  const leaves = ranked.filter(([, , effect]) => effect);
  if (leaves.length > 1 && rank(leaves[0]) === rank(leaves[1])) throw new Ambiguous(signal, leaves.map(([match]) => match));
  return leaves[0] ?? ranked[0];
}

export function greedy(vector, signal) {
  for (const { pattern, trajectory } of vector.trie.values()) {
    const match = pattern.apply(signal);
    if (match) return [[match, trajectory, trajectory.effect ?? null]];
  }

  return [];
}

export function scope(vector, signal) {
  const scope = [];

  for (const { pattern, trajectory } of vector.trie.values()) {
    const match = pattern.apply(signal);
    if (match) scope.push([match, trajectory, trajectory.effect ?? null]);
  }

  return scope;
}
#+END_SRC

The live file before this tangle is the 27-line =match.js= quoted in the terrain (=feed= returned =matches.find(([, , effect]) => effect) ?? matches[0]=); =greedy= and =scope= are unchanged. =rank(…) ?? 0= keeps the existing =match.test.js= cases, which pass bare Signals (no =type=) as matches.

+    this.signal = signal;

+}

=traverse= catches only =NotFound= (=dispatch.js:28-31=) — an =Ambiguous= propagates to the caller, which is the law: ambiguity is rendered, never swallowed. Ghost's =fail()= learns the code in M4.

Run: =deno test -A --no-check subsystems/typology/tests/gestalten/steer/*.test.js subsystems/typology/tests/{signal,signature,vector}.test.js= and the two snapshot suites with =SNAPSHOT_HOT= unset — a snapshot that moves is a FINDING (a serialized match carried a stamp), reported, not regenerated.
** M3 — three ways: cut · captured · chrome
beef's three ways, 10-06: /"our three names are console captured and chrome. console is inline one off commands. captured is interactive ongoing but still inline. chrome is fullscreen captured ongoing."/ → /"cut captured chrome"/. As code: =view.cut.print(data, Component)= is one frame into scrollback (a doctor, a listing, the fallback =Effect=); =view.cut.hold(data, Component)= is a one-off that holds the input inline until it closes (a picker, a confirm, a wizard). =view.capture(data, faces)= is the long-lived root of a session, whose face is =captured= (inline, the dock under the scrollback) or =chrome= (=<Fullscreen>=, the crown and the spine). =shift(face)= swaps the face in place, and the capture carries its own =cut= that paints into its slot. The comp (=** the comp=) draws all three. The control is a typology =Controller= (F10): a component receives =controller=, ends with =controller.stdout.close(result)=, cancels with =controller.kill("SIGTERM", why)=; the view resolves =result=, or ={ aborted: true }= after a stop. =Span.close= gains the optional =data= so a close can carry the result the way every other mark carries its data. =BufferControl · buffer.render · hijack · scroll.*= dissolve; =--buffer= becomes a capture worn as chrome, with =Slot= its only face: any verb painted fullscreen, held until esc.

+import { Controller, Span } from "@vivalence/typology";
+import { Fullscreen } from "./components/Fullscreen.jsx";
+
+const controlled = (name) => new Controller({ stdout: new Span(`view/${name}`) });
+
+const live = (controller) => controller.status.is(["IDLE", "RUNNING", "PAUSED", "STOPPING"]);
+
+const settle = (controller, release, resolve) => {
+  let result;
+  const untap = controller.stdout.pipe.tap((record) => {
+    if (record.span !== controller.stdout.id) return;
+    if (record.verb === "close") result = record.data;
+    if (record.verb === "stop") controller.stdout.close();
+  });
+  controller.settled.then((reflection) => {
+    untap();
+    release();
+    resolve(reflection.code === "DONE" ? result : { aborted: true, ...(result ?? {}) });
+  });
+};
+
+export const view = {
+  cut: {
+    print(data, Component) {
+      const instance = render(
+        <Static items={[<Component {...data} />]}>
+          {(item, index) => <Box key={index}>{item}</Box>}
+        </Static>,
+      );
+      setTimeout(() => instance.unmount(), 0);
+      return instance.waitUntilExit();
+    },
+    hold(data, Component, controller = controlled(Component.name)) {
+      return new Promise((resolve) => {
+        const instance = render(<Component {...data} controller={controller} />);
+        instance.waitUntilExit().then(() => live(controller) && controller.kill("SIGKILL", "ctrl-c"));
+        settle(controller, () => instance.unmount(), resolve);
+        controller.stdout.open();
+      });
+    },
+  },
+  capture(data, faces, controller = controlled("capture")) {
+    let slot = null;
+    let held = false;
+    let face = data.face ?? "captured";
+    const shift = (next) => {
+      if (!faces[next] || next === face) return;
+      face = next;
+      instance.rerender(<Root />);
+    };
+    function Root() {
+      const Face = faces[face];
+      const body = <Face {...data} controller={controller} slot={slot} live={held} face={face} shift={shift} />;
+      return face === "chrome" ? <Fullscreen>{body}</Fullscreen> : body;
+    }
+    const paint = (child, holding = false) => {
+      slot = child;
+      held = holding;
+      instance.rerender(<Root />);
+    };
+    const instance = render(<Root />, { exitOnCtrlC: false });
+    controller.stdout.open();
+    const settled = new Promise((resolve) => settle(controller, () => instance.unmount(), resolve));
+    return {
+      controller,
+      settled,
+      shift,
+      clear: () => paint(null),
+      get face() {
+        return face;
+      },
+      cut: {
+        print(inner, Component) {
+          paint(<Component {...inner} />);
+          return Promise.resolve();
+        },
+        hold(inner, Component, control = controlled(Component.name)) {
+          return new Promise((resolve) => {
+            paint(<Component {...inner} controller={control} />, true);
+            settle(control, () => paint(null), resolve);
+            control.stdout.open();
+          });
+        },
+      },
+    };
+  },
+};
#+END_SRC

The machine behind =settle=: =open= IDLE→RUNNING at mount; =close= RUNNING→DONE resolves the result; =kill("SIGTERM")= marks =stop= RUNNING→STOPPING and the view answers it with a =close= → STOPPED → ={ aborted: true }= (=schematics/primitives/controller.js:13-20=). The Controller's own tap is registered first (=controller.js:16=), so the status has moved before the view's tap reads the record. A capture returns the same =cut= the view has — =print= and =hold= — painting into its slot; that is what =hijack= did with a second name. =shift(face)= swaps the face in place: the controller, the slot and the session survive it, and only the chrome wraps itself in =<Fullscreen>=, so the escape pair is written on the way in and taken back on the way out. Two ink facts bind it (=ink/5.2.1/build/render.js:14= =exitOnCtrlC: true= by default): a plain =render= keeps ink's ctrl-c, and ink's exit KILLS the controller so the verb resolves ={ aborted: true }= instead of hanging on a component ink already unmounted; a capture turns ctrl-c off — the face owns it (the dock: stop the running activity, again to leave). And every active =useInput= hears every key, so the capture tells its face when a live component holds the slot (=live=): the face's own keys stand down while a picker or a confirm is painted.

#+BEGIN_SRC jsx :tangle ../../subsystems/sheets/components/Fullscreen.jsx :mkdirp yes
import React, { useEffect } from "react";
import { Box, useStdout } from "ink";

export function Fullscreen({ children }) {
  const { stdout } = useStdout();
  useEffect(() => {
    stdout.write("\x1b[?1049h");
    return () => stdout.write("\x1b[?1049l");
  }, [stdout]);
  return <Box flexDirection="column">{children}</Box>;
}
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC jsx :tangle ../../subsystems/sheets/components/Slot.jsx :mkdirp yes
import React from "react";
import { Box, useInput } from "ink";

export function Slot({ slot, live, controller }) {
  useInput(
    (_, key) => {
      if (key.escape) controller.kill("SIGTERM", "esc");
    },
    { isActive: !live },
  );
  return <Box flexDirection="column">{slot}</Box>;
}
#+END_SRC

=Fullscreen= is the ONE home of the escape pair (the law); it binds no height — =codemap/design.md=: /"never =minHeight = stdout.rows= (full repaint per key)"/. =Slot= is the =--buffer= capture's only face: whatever the last effect painted, esc ends the session — unless a live component holds the slot, whose own esc is the one that counts. The three kit components that answered a =buffer= learn the controller (=JsonTree= has no caller today — =mod.js:3= imports it and never renders it — but its =buffer?.release?.()= would hang any future =render=, and the grep gate below catches the form):

The law the view stands on — =close= carries the result, a stop or a kill resolves ={ aborted: true }=, =emit= resolves one frame — is testable today without a terminal: a component that closes or stops ITSELF on mount needs no keys. The pass added it /(pass: the testing assessment said no view test was possible; every path that takes no input is)/:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/sheets/tests/view.cut.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import { React, Text, useEffect, view } from "../mod.jsx";

const IN_PROCESS = { sanitizeOps: false, sanitizeResources: false };

function Closes({ controller, result }) {
  useEffect(() => void controller.stdout.close(result), []);
  return React.createElement(Text, null, "closing");
}

function Stops({ controller }) {
  useEffect(() => void controller.kill("SIGTERM", "stopped on mount"), []);
  return React.createElement(Text, null, "stopping");
}

function Aborts({ controller }) {
  useEffect(() => void controller.kill("SIGKILL", "killed on mount"), []);
  return React.createElement(Text, null, "aborting");
}

Deno.test({
  name: "view.cut.hold: a component that closes with data resolves that data",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    assertEquals(await view.cut.hold({ result: { chosen: "home" } }, Closes), { chosen: "home" });
  },
});

Deno.test({
  name: "view.cut.hold: a stop resolves aborted",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    assertEquals(await view.cut.hold({}, Stops), { aborted: true });
  },
});

Deno.test({
  name: "view.cut.hold: a kill resolves aborted",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    assertEquals(await view.cut.hold({}, Aborts), { aborted: true });
  },
});

Deno.test({
  name: "view.cut.print: one frame, resolved",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    await view.cut.print({ children: "a frame" }, Text);
  },
});
#+END_SRC

Ghost's render middleware (=mod.js:85-124=), rewritten: one =ctx.view= with =cut= and =capture= (=capture= is what the session opens, M11; inside a capture a second one is refused by name). The effect is painted BEFORE an own capture is held, never after its root unmounted. A capture arrives either from =--buffer= (made here, worn as chrome) or from the session that called =ctx.call= (M11, carried on the inner ctx as =ctx.capture=); a carried capture is not held and not killed here, because its owner does both.

The gate is a walk of each wizard plus the grep in the milestone row; the mapping =release(opts) ≙ stdout.close(opts)= stays QUESTIONED (F10) until beef walks it.
** M4 — references · yap · choice
A reference is a literal opened at mount, so the mount moves to the pinhole, before the match (F14): the cwd probe (=mod.js:73-82=) moves out of the middleware into the pinhole, the pinhole mounts whatever instance is selected (memoized — =lifecycle.mount= caches by object identity, =paladin/lifecycle/index.js:8=; the middleware's own mount is the same memo) ONLY when the first word is no command noun, or is =help= — a reference or a sentence needs the instance, a command never does, and a pre-mount under a command breaks every verb that re-targets: =instance/doctor <other>= sets its mount then mounts the SAME =paladin.instance= object, so the memo hands back the pre-mounted instance and the first =.env= stays claimed in the =instance= stratum (=populate.environment=, =paladin/lifecycle/populate.js:120-126=). A mount that fails opens no references and says nothing — the verb that needs the instance says it. Then =references.open= hangs one literal per mode the recipe seats (=daemon.kernel[].reference= is =/mode/<type>/<slug>= after =resolve.ground=, =paladin/lifecycle/resolve.js:93=) plus =/home= from the credentials row (M9; =null= until it lands), and =yap.open= hangs the root =(.*)=. The ranked =feed= (M2) keeps every command ahead of the yap.

The =home= constant is M9's seam (=await paladin.ledger.credentials.home(origin)=); until then it is =null= and =/home= is not an edge. Middleware order stays the law — span → catch → =ctx.call= → cwd mount → render — the probe that SETS the cwd stratum merely runs before the match, as every flag's =paladin.env.set= already does (=mod.js:176=).

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/trajectories/references.js :mkdirp yes
import { cast, v } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { open as shell } from "../belt/session.js";

const SEAT = /^\/mode\/([^/]+)\/([^/]+)$/;

export const rows = (instance) =>
  instance.daemons.flatMap((daemon) =>
    (daemon.kernel ?? []).flatMap((entry) => {
      const [, type, slug] = String(entry?.reference ?? "").match(SEAT) ?? [];
      return slug ? [{ daemon: daemon.manifest.slug, type, slug }] : [];
    }),
  );

export const nature = (trajectory, row, all) =>
  trajectory.trie.has(row.slug) || all.filter((held) => held.slug === row.slug).length > 1 ? `/${row.daemon}/${row.slug}` : `/${row.slug}`;

const schema = v.object({ turn: v.string().desc("the rest of the line — the mode's first turn").optional() });

export function open(trajectory, instance, home = null) {
  const all = rows(instance);
  if (home) {
    const { type, slug } = cast.lookup(home);
    const row = all.find((held) => held.type === type && held.slug === slug) ?? null;
    trajectory.open(
      { nature: "/home", valence: `your home mode (${home}); the rest of the line is its first turn`, schema, reference: row },
      (ctx) => reference(ctx, row, home),
    );
  }
  for (const row of all) {
    trajectory.open(
      { nature: nature(trajectory, row, all), valence: `open ${row.daemon}'s ${row.type}/${row.slug}; the rest of the line is its first turn`, schema, reference: row },
      (ctx) => reference(ctx, row),
    );
  }
  return all;
}

export async function reference(ctx, row, home = null) {
  if (!row) return (ctx.effect = { error: `home: ${home} is not a mode of this instance — viva help` });
  const turn = ctx.signal.params.join(" ");
  if (ctx.session) {
    ctx.rendered = true;
    return (ctx.effect = await ctx.session.seat(row, turn));
  }
  if (!ctx.interactive) return (ctx.effect = { route: "reference", receiver: row, payload: turn });
  ctx.effect = await shell(ctx, row, turn);
}
#+END_SRC

Until M11, =belt/session.js= is a one-line stub that answers the Resolution (tangled under M11, replaced whole there) — the import stands from M4 on and the boundary boots green. Inside a session the reference marks =ctx.rendered=: the dock is its surface, and the inner render middleware would otherwise paint its ={ seated }= effect into the slot. =cast.lookup("@education/home/aprende")= → ={ owner, type, slug }= (=gestalten/cast/primitives.js:24=). A mode slug that is already an edge of the trajectory — a verb, a branch like =instance=, the =home= literal (opened first) — or held by two daemons, lives under its daemon: =viva education/home=; the trie decides, no list of nouns /(pass 3: the first draft kept a =VERBS= set)/.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/trajectories/yap.js :mkdirp yes
import { NotFound, Signal, v } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { search } from "@vivalence/sheets";
import { pick } from "../belt/index.js";
import { census } from "./help.js";
import { Choice } from "./Choice.jsx";

export const CHOICES = ["send to home", "retry search", "open as turn"];

export const natures = (trajectory, under = null) => {
  const rows = census(trajectory).filter((row) => row.nature !== "<sentence>" && (!under || row.nature.startsWith(`${under}/`)));
  return { label: under ?? "nature", rows, keys: ["nature", "valence"], facets: [], columns: ["nature", "valence"], identifier: (row) => row.nature };
};

export const nearest = (trajectory, word) => {
  if (trajectory.trie.has(word)) return natures(trajectory, word).rows.map((row) => row.nature);
  const lens = natures(trajectory);
  return search.seek(search.init({ rows: lens.rows, keys: ["nature"] }), word).matches.map((at) => lens.rows[at].nature);
};

export function open(trajectory, home = null) {
  trajectory.open(
    { nature: "/(.*)", valence: "anything else — a turn for your home mode, or a search when it is one word", schema: v.object({}) },
    (ctx) => yap(ctx, trajectory, home),
  );
}

export async function yap(ctx, trajectory, home) {
  const words = [...ctx.signal.absolute, ...ctx.signal.params];
  const text = words.join(" ");
  if (ctx.session) {
    ctx.rendered = true;
    return (ctx.effect = await ctx.session.turn(text));
  }
  if (!ctx.interactive && words.length === 1) throw new NotFound(ctx.signal);
  if (!ctx.interactive) return (ctx.effect = { error: `no handler for '${text}' — ${home ? `viva home ${text} sends it to ${home}` : "try: viva help"}` });
  const [word] = words;
  const under = words.length === 1 && trajectory.trie.has(word) ? word : null;
  const near = words.length === 1 ? nearest(trajectory, word) : [];
  if (near.length) {
    const chosen = await pick(ctx, natures(trajectory, under), under ? "" : word);
    if (chosen?.aborted) return (ctx.effect = { aborted: true });
    if (chosen?.row) return (ctx.effect = await ctx.call(Signal.tokens(chosen.row.nature)));
  }
  const choice = await ctx.view.cut.hold({ text, home, near, choices: home ? CHOICES : CHOICES.slice(1, 2) }, Choice);
  if (choice?.aborted) return (ctx.effect = { aborted: true });
  if (choice === "retry search") {
    const chosen = await pick(ctx, natures(trajectory));
    return (ctx.effect = chosen?.row ? await ctx.call(Signal.tokens(chosen.row.nature)) : { aborted: true });
  }
  ctx.effect = await ctx.call(["home", ...words]);
}
#+END_SRC

F8 as written: /"send to $home"/ and /"open as turn"/ both dispatch =home <words>= until beef says what a stashed turn is; without a =home= the screen offers only the search. =pick= with one match answers headlessly (=belt/pick.js:16=), so =viva flashc= reaches =flashcards= without a frame; =viva fl= with two opens the picker preset to =fl=. A bare noun that is an edge — =viva instance= — lists its own children and opens the picker on them, never on a fuzzy neighbourhood (=instances/*= stays out of =instance='s list; =viva instanc=, no edge, still searches) /(pass 3)/. =search= here is the sheets state module (=state/search.js=), the same fold =pick= runs. =natures()= drops the =<sentence>= row — =help.js= prints the remainder by that name (below), so a filter on =(.*)= would list the yap in its own picker.

#+BEGIN_SRC jsx :tangle ../../systems/ghost/trajectories/Choice.jsx :mkdirp yes
import { Box, React, Select, Text, theme, useInput } from "@vivalence/sheets";

export function Choice({ text, home, near, choices, controller }) {
  useInput((_, key) => {
    if (key.escape) controller.kill("SIGTERM", "esc");
  });
  return (
    <Box flexDirection="column">
      <Text>
        <Text color={theme.dim}>no handler for </Text>
        <Text bold>{text}</Text>
      </Text>
      {home ? <Text color={theme.dim}>home: {home}</Text> : <Text color={theme.warn}>your identity carries no HOMED trait — nothing to send to</Text>}
      {near.length ? <Text color={theme.dim}>near: {near.join("  ")}</Text> : null}
      <Select items={choices} done={(value) => controller.stdout.close(value)} />
      <Text color={theme.dim}>↑↓ move  ⏎ choose  esc cancel</Text>
    </Box>
  );
}
#+END_SRC

=help= learns the two new kinds of row: the census carries the pattern's =reference=, the remainder prints as =<sentence>=, and the listing groups references under =modes=:

In =frame.steps= the steps are PATTERNS (=trie.fold= pushes =pattern=, =trie.js:18=), so =step.type= is the probe's kind. The remainder row still exists for =viva help= to say what a bare sentence does.

The miss, headless, as the sandbox walked it — a root word is a NotFound whose near list is the yap's own fuzzy search; a deeper miss lists its siblings under the =prefix/= /(pass: the first draft's =startsWith(prefix)= listed =instances/*= under =instance/nope=, and its yap answered a one-word miss with an error effect — exit 1, no hint — which reddened =help.wet.test.js='s /"unknown nature hints at help"/ and contradicted this milestone's own gate)/:

$ viva bogus                  → ghost: no handler for bogus · try: viva help                     exit 127

$ viva instance/nope          → ghost: no handler for instance/nope · near: instance/use  instance/create  instance/init  instance/run  …   exit 127

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/references.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assertEquals, assertRejects } from "@std/assert";
import { NotFound, Path, steer, Signal, Vector } from "@vivalence/typology";
import * as references from "../trajectories/references.js";
import * as yap from "../trajectories/yap.js";
import { census } from "../trajectories/help.js";

const instance = {
  daemons: [
    { manifest: { slug: "education" }, kernel: [{ module: "@education/home/aprende", reference: new Path("/mode/home/aprende") }, { module: "@education/game/flashcard", reference: "/mode/game/flashcards" }, { module: "@education/tool/help", reference: "/mode/tool/help" }] },
    { manifest: { slug: "chess" }, kernel: [{ module: "@chess/board/play", reference: "/mode/board/play" }, { module: "@chess/game/flashcards", reference: "/mode/game/flashcards" }, "./typeless.viva.js"] },
  ],
};

const strategy = (carry, effect) => async (context) => {
  await carry(context, async (ctx) => {
    const result = await effect(ctx);
    ctx.effect ??= result;
  });
  return context.effect;
};

const run = (trajectory, argv, carried = {}) => {
  const signal = new Signal(argv);
  return steer.dispatch.invoke(trajectory, signal, strategy)({ signal, effect: null, interactive: false, ...carried });
};

Deno.test("references: one edge per seated mode; a verb or a shared slug lives under its daemon; a typeless entry opens nothing", () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  trajectory.open("/help", () => "help");
  references.open(trajectory, instance);
  const natures = census(trajectory).map((row) => row.nature);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("aprende"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("play"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("education/help"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("education/flashcards"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("chess/flashcards"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("flashcards"), false);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("typeless"), false);
});

Deno.test("references: headless, a reference resolves to its row and the rest of the line", async () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  references.open(trajectory, instance);
  const effect = await run(trajectory, ["aprende", "what", "did", "i", "miss"]);
  assertEquals(effect, { route: "reference", receiver: { daemon: "education", type: "home", slug: "aprende" }, payload: "what did i miss" });
});

Deno.test("references: /home resolves the identity's trait to a seated mode, or says it is not one", async () => {
  const homed = new Vector();
  references.open(homed, instance, "@education/home/aprende");
  assertEquals((await run(homed, ["home", "hi"])).receiver.slug, "aprende");
  const stray = new Vector();
  references.open(stray, instance, "@education/home/gone");
  assertEquals(typeof (await run(stray, ["home"])).error, "string");
});

Deno.test("yap: a command still wins the match over the root remainder", async () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  trajectory.branch("/instance").open("/use", (ctx) => ({ used: ctx.signal.params }));
  yap.open(trajectory);
  assertEquals(await run(trajectory, ["instance/use", "italian"]), { used: ["italian"] });
});

Deno.test("yap: a sentence in a pipe is an error naming the way out; one word is a NotFound whose near list the shell prints", async () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  trajectory.branch("/instance").open("/use", () => "use");
  references.open(trajectory, instance);
  yap.open(trajectory, "@education/home/aprende");
  const sentence = await run(trajectory, ["what", "did", "i", "get", "wrong"]);
  assertEquals(sentence.error.startsWith("no handler for 'what did i get wrong'"), true);
  assertEquals(sentence.error.includes("viva home what did i get wrong"), true);
  await assertRejects(() => run(trajectory, ["apre"]), NotFound);
  assertEquals(yap.nearest(trajectory, "apre").includes("aprende"), true);
});

Deno.test("yap: inside a session, a sentence is a turn and a reference reseats", async () => {
  const seen = [];
  const session = { turn: async (text) => (seen.push(["turn", text]), { turned: text }), seat: async (row, first) => (seen.push(["seat", row.slug, first]), { seated: row.slug }) };
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  references.open(trajectory, instance);
  yap.open(trajectory);
  assertEquals(await run(trajectory, ["tell", "me", "more"], { session }), { turned: "tell me more" });
  assertEquals(await run(trajectory, ["play", "e4"], { session }), { seated: "play" });
  assertEquals(seen, [["turn", "tell me more"], ["seat", "play", "e4"]]);
});

Deno.test("references: the trie decides collisions — a mode slugged like a verb, or like home, lives under its daemon", () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  trajectory.branch("/instance").open("/use", () => "use");
  const held = { daemons: [{ manifest: { slug: "edu" }, kernel: [{ module: "@edu/x/instance", reference: "/mode/x/instance" }, { module: "@edu/home/home", reference: "/mode/home/home" }, { module: "@edu/y/free", reference: "/mode/y/free" }] }] };
  references.open(trajectory, held, "@edu/home/home");
  const natures = census(trajectory).map((row) => row.nature);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("instance/use"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("edu/instance"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("edu/home"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("home"), true);
  assertEquals(natures.includes("free"), true);
});

Deno.test("yap: a bare noun that is an edge lists its own children, never a fuzzy neighbourhood", () => {
  const trajectory = new Vector();
  trajectory.branch("/instance").open("/use", () => "use").open("/run", () => "run");
  trajectory.branch("/instances").open("/list", () => "list");
  yap.open(trajectory);
  assertEquals(yap.nearest(trajectory, "instance"), ["instance/use", "instance/run"]);
  assertEquals(yap.nearest(trajectory, "instanc").includes("instances/list"), true);
  assertEquals(yap.natures(trajectory, "instance").rows.length, 2);
});
#+END_SRC

** M5 — lighthouse/localhost
beef: /"a new localhost lighthouse that just has one user, and automatically signs up everyone to anything."/ Copy right: the whole multiplayer attachment is grafted and two leaves are re-opened — =/signup= and =/login= answer the one identity whatever is typed (=Vector.open= on an existing edge replaces its effect, =vector.js:41=). Verify · refresh · logout · enroll stay multiplayer's: the daemons still authenticate a real JWT (=R/prototypes/lighthouse.js:63=) and the roster is bearer-walled since #251, so "signs up everyone to everything" is the handshake every daemon already performs on first contact. The provider is multiplayer's too — the package is one, relative imports inside =commons= are its own.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../commons/lighthouses/localhost/attachment/auth.js :mkdirp yes
import { Vector } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { wrap } from "@vivalence/runtime";
import { signer } from "../../multiplayer/gestalten/identity.js";

const SLUG = "localhost";

const card = (identity) => ({ id: identity.id, slug: identity.slug, traits: identity.traits, trait: identity.trait });

const only = async (ctx) => {
  const held = await ctx.entities.identity.findOne({ slug: SLUG });
  if (held) return held;
  const identity = ctx.entities.identity.create({ slug: SLUG, traits: ["ADMIN"] });
  await ctx.entities.em.flush();
  return identity;
};

const admit = async (input, ctx) => {
  const identity = await only(ctx);
  const sign = signer(ctx);
  const tokens = { access: await sign.access(identity), refresh: await sign.refresh(identity) };
  wrap(identity).assign({ authentication: { provider: "localhost", tokens } }, { merge: true, mergeObjectProperties: true });
  await ctx.entities.em.flush();
  return { status: "SUCCESS", authority: tokens, identity: card(identity) };
};

export const auth = new Vector().open("/signup", admit).open("/login", admit);
#+END_SRC

=graft= deep-copies the multiplayer tree at graft time (=vector.js:52=; =codemap/typology.md=: /"graft(vector) deep-COPIES frozen at graft time"/), so the second graft of =auth= onto the copied =/auth= branch replaces two effects on the COPY — multiplayer's own Vector is untouched, and an instance declaring both lighthouses holds two trees. The recipe: hello-world's, re-pointed, on offset ports, with the launch word this quest adds (M8) and the harness testbed by its registry identifiers — =@commons/chaosmonkey/oracle= (HARNESSED; its harness one short system line, =commons/playground/chaosmonkey/oracle/harness.js:4-12=) and the =vision= its emitter reaches for (=oracle/emitter.js:13=); playground's kernel names both (=commons/instances/playground/instance.viva.js:46-47=). NOT the hello-world demo, found at the fold: OPEN known issue =hello-world-mode-hook-reads-ctx-user-before-authorize= — the demo's aperture hook reads =ctx.user= before =authorize= (=commons/instances/hello-world/mode.viva.js:29-33=), every route of the mode answers 500, the dossier marks the daemon =error= and the client refuses it (M10). The recipe takes the demo when that issue closes; its ruling is beef's.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../commons/instances/localhost/instance.viva.js :mkdirp yes
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { v } from "@vivalence/typology";

export const manifest = { type: "instance", slug: "localhost", version: "0.0.1" };

export const datamap = { module: "@commons/datamap/libsql" };

export const lighthouse = {
  module: "@commons/lighthouse/localhost",
  statics: { remote: () => paladin.env.get("PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE") },
};

export const hallucinators = [
  {
    module: "@commons/hallucinator/anthropic",
    secrets: { key: () => paladin.secret.get("SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY") },
  },
  {
    module: "@commons/hallucinator/openrouter",
    secrets: { key: () => paladin.secret.get("SECRET_VIVA_OPENROUTER_API_KEY") },
  },
];

export const daemons = [
  {
    manifest: { type: "daemon", slug: "hello", version: "0.0.1" },
    kernel: ["@commons/chaosmonkey/oracle", "@commons/chaosmonkey/vision"],
  },
];

export const runtime = {
  manifest: { slug: "runtime" },
  statics: {
    serve: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE"),
    remote: () => paladin.env.get("PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE"),
    launch: "inline",
  },
};

export const clients = [
  {
    manifest: { type: "client", slug: "anima" },
    statics: { serve: () => paladin.env.get("VIVA_CLIENT_ANIMA_SERVE") },
  },
];

export const services = [
  {
    manifest: { type: "lighthouse", slug: "localhost" },
    module: "@commons/lighthouse/localhost",
    secrets: { jwt: () => paladin.secret.get("SECRET_VIVA_JWT") },
  },
];

export const environment = v.environment({
  VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN: v.url().desc("Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Offset from hello-world's 2501 and playground's 2502 so all three can run at once.").default("http://localhost:2503").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_CLIENT_ANIMA_ORIGIN: v.url().desc("Scheme and authority the anima browser client is reachable at.").default("http://localhost:1796").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE: v.url().desc("Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.").default("${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/").group("addresses"),
  VIVA_CLIENT_ANIMA_SERVE: v.url().desc("Where the anima browser client serves.").default("${VIVA_CLIENT_ANIMA_ORIGIN}/").group("addresses"),
  PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE: v.url().desc("Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.").default("${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}").group("addresses"),
  PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE: v.url().desc("Lighthouse address as CONSUMED. Unset, paladin computes this runtime's reach + the hosting service's seat.").group("addresses").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_JWT: v.string({ minLength: 24 }).desc("Lighthouse signing secret. Minted at first init; rotate with: openssl rand -base64 24").default(() => btoa(String.fromCharCode(...crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(24))))).group("keys"),
  SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: v.string().desc("Anthropic key. Declare either provider, both, or neither — a key left blank leaves its hallucinator dormant.").group("keys").optional(),
  SECRET_VIVA_OPENROUTER_API_KEY: v.string().desc("OpenRouter key. The alternative provider. With neither key the daemon attaches no hallucinator and the oracle has no voice.").group("keys").optional(),
});
#+END_SRC

A =launch= declared before M8 lands is an extra static — =Statics= allows additional properties (=instance.js:6=) — so this recipe boots on today's tree too. The test rides the one lighthouse rig:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../commons/lighthouses/localhost/tests/auth.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { specimen } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { lighthouse } from "@vivalence/runtime/scenarios";

specimen.describe("Lighthouse localhost — one identity, every door", () => {
  let scenario;

  specimen.beforeAll(async () => {
    scenario = await lighthouse.create({ module: "@commons/lighthouse/localhost" });
  });

  specimen.afterAll(async () => {
    await scenario.close();
  });

  specimen.it("two signups with two names are one identity", async () => {
    const first = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/signup", { username: "finn", password: "whatever" });
    const second = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/signup", { username: "kiro", password: "else" });
    specimen.expect(first.identity.id).toBe(second.identity.id);
    specimen.expect(first.identity.slug).toBe("localhost");
  });

  specimen.it("a login with anything verifies as that identity", async () => {
    const login = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/login", { username: "x", password: "y" });
    const verified = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/verify", { access: login.authority.access });
    specimen.expect(verified.identity.slug).toBe("localhost");
  });

  specimen.it("refresh and logout are multiplayer's", async () => {
    const login = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/login", {});
    const refreshed = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/refresh", { refresh: login.authority.refresh });
    specimen.expect(refreshed.access).toBeDefined();
    const out = await scenario.conn.call("/auth/logout", { refresh: login.authority.refresh });
    specimen.expect(out.status).toBe("SUCCESS");
  });
});
#+END_SRC
