** M6 — the client structures move
F9 ruled: ghost is the source, anima imports from it. What moves is what is pure already (=codemap/anima.md= DOGMA: structures are pure; the two violations it names — =localStorage= in =hydrate=, the =Connection= built in the dossier — are the two seams this milestone opens). What stays in anima is the browser: =stores/bridge= (layout, DOM, =localStorage=), =stores/box= (AudioContext), =prototypes/dictation.js= (the microphone), =prototypes/todo/=. The =$telemetry= logger (=src/telemetry/logger.js=: a Pipe, a Span, a chronicle — pure) travels so the moved files keep their taps; anima re-exports it so its inspector reads the same channel. =stores/bridge/dock.js= is pure geometry read by =entities/terminal.js=; it travels as =client/dock.js= and anima's bridge re-exports it.

The predicate reads CONTENT, not presence: nanostores' =effect= runs at once on registration (=nanostores/1.0.1/effect/index.js=: =run()= after the listens), and =$authority · $identity= start as =map({})= — truthy — so the old =authority || identity= wrote a row of two empty objects on every first load without a login (harmless in a browser, a schematic fault in the ledger's store). A write that throws is a span fault on the authority entry, never an unhandled rejection; the body stays a block, because a callback that RETURNS a function is nanostores' cleanup for the next run. Writes are still partial mid-flight (=login= sets =authority= before =identity=, =logout= nulls them one at a time) — the ledger's store skips a row without both (M9).

The store shape, with anima's browser store beside the ledger's (M9):

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/client/stores/store.js :mkdirp yes
export const browser = (key) => ({
  read: () => {
    const held = localStorage.getItem(key);
    return held ? JSON.parse(held) : null;
  },
  write: (value) => localStorage.setItem(key, JSON.stringify(value)),
  remove: () => localStorage.removeItem(key),
});

export const memory = (held = new Map(), key = "authority") => ({
  read: () => held.get(key) ?? null,
  write: (value) => held.set(key, value),
  remove: () => held.delete(key),
});
#+END_SRC

=browser= names =localStorage= and lives in the client package — it is anima's store, exported for anima, never called inside the structures; the purity grep (M6 gate) runs over the STRUCTURES — =entities · prototypes · belt · stores/{lighthouse,terminals}.js= — and leaves out the two named seams, =stores/store.js= (the browser's store) and =reach.js= (the one place a =Connection= is built: the first draft's gate over the whole =client/= went red on its own seam). Second seam, the dossier receives its transport from the lighthouse (=reach=) instead of building one (=dossier.js:57-65=):

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/client/reach.js :mkdirp yes
import { Connection, shard } from "@vivalence/typology";

export const remote = (url, lighthouse) => {
  const multiplex = shard.transmitter.multiplex({ authority: lighthouse.$authority });
  const connection = new Connection(url, shard.transmitter.retry(multiplex, { maxRetries: 2 }))
    .use(shard.track.span((call) => call.request.url.pathname, lighthouse.channel))
    .use(shard.track.request())
    .use(shard.track.fault())
    .use(shard.connection.timeout(8000));
  return { connection, release: multiplex.close, $state: multiplex.$state };
};
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/client/lighthouse.store.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import { Connection, Url } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { Lighthouse, hydrate, memory } from "../../client/mod.js";

const dead = async (ctx) => {
  ctx.response.status = 0;
};

Deno.test("hydrate: a store's row lands on the lighthouse, a change writes back, a logout removes", async () => {
  const held = new Map([["authority", { authority: { access: "a", refresh: "r" }, identity: { id: "1", slug: "beef", traits: [], trait: {} } }]]);
  const lighthouse = new Lighthouse(new Connection(new Url("http://x"), dead));
  await hydrate(lighthouse, memory(held));
  assertEquals(lighthouse.isAuthorized, true);
  assertEquals(lighthouse.identity.slug, "beef");
  lighthouse.$authority.set({ access: "b", refresh: "r" });
  assertEquals(held.get("authority").authority.access, "b");
  lighthouse.logout();
  assertEquals(held.has("authority"), false);
});

Deno.test("hydrate: an empty store leaves the lighthouse unauthorized and writes nothing", async () => {
  const held = new Map();
  const lighthouse = new Lighthouse(new Connection(new Url("http://x"), dead));
  await hydrate(lighthouse, memory(held));
  assertEquals(lighthouse.isAuthorized, false);
  assertEquals(held.size, 0);
});
#+END_SRC

=memory= is exported beside =browser= from =stores/store.js= for this test and for ghost's own (M10). Run: =deno test -A --no-check systems/ghost/tests/client/lighthouse.store.test.js systems/anima/tests/terminals.remote.test.js systems/anima/tests/lifecycle-vector.test.js=.
** M7 — the inline transport
The transport exists (terrain 12). What a hosted runtime needs from it is that a client which stops reading ENDS the server's generator — else every =/subscribe= a shell opened keeps spinning inside the ghost after esc. The fold's re-read found the first draft's answer wrong twice over: a caller's =AbortSignal= never reaches a typology =Request= (its constructor reads no =signal=, =prototypes/request.js:5-24=; =Connection.fetch= spreads the option in and it is dropped, =connection.js:80-88=), so no change to =inline= alone can carry it; and =Response.stream= (=prototypes/response.js:15-25=) builds its =ReadableStream= with no =cancel=, so a reader's cancel never returns the source iterator. The working path is the second: =stream.return()= → =sse.frames='s =finally= → =reader.cancel()= (=belt/sse.js:46-49=) → the response stream's =cancel= → =iterator.return()= → the server generator's =finally=. =sse.encode= already does exactly this (=belt/sse.js:18-20=); =Response.stream= learns it. The =signal= on the inline =Request= stays — the request's OWN signal, so a =timeout= shard's =request.abort()= reaches the server's =raw.signal= the way =fetcher='s does (=transmitter.js:261=). The dropped caller signal outlives this quest: known issue =connection-stream-drops-the-callers-signal=.

#+BEGIN_SRC diff
// subsystems/typology/prototypes/response.js:15
   stream(source) {
     const iterator = source[Symbol.asyncIterator]();
     this.body = new ReadableStream({
       async pull(controller) {
         const { value, done } = await iterator.next();
         if (done) return controller.close();
         controller.enqueue(value instanceof Uint8Array ? value : encoder.encode(String(value)));
       },
+      async cancel() {
+        await iterator.return?.();
+      },
     });
     return this;
   }
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC diff
// subsystems/typology/gestalten/shard/transmitter.js:218
 export const inline = (serve) => async (ctx) => {
   const req = new Request(ctx.request.url.absolute, {
     method: ctx.request.method,
     headers: {
       "content-type": "application/json",
       ...Object.fromEntries(ctx.request.headers),
     },
     body:
       ctx.request.method !== "GET" && ctx.request.body !== undefined
         ? JSON.stringify(ctx.request.body)
         : undefined,
+    signal: ctx.request.signal,
   });
#+END_SRC

=Request.signal= is a lazy getter minting the request's own =AbortController= (=prototypes/request.js:26=); =shape.http= hands the raw request to the context (=http.js:8= =raw: req=), where the reactive shards read =raw.signal=. The iterator =Response.stream= holds is =sse.lines(source)= (=publish=, =response.js:27-31=) — a generator =for await=-ing the handler's output, so its =return()= returns the handler's iterator in turn; =shard.datamap.inject='s wrapper forwards =return= (=shard/datamap.js:22=).

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/typology/tests/gestalten/shard/transmitter.inline.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { specimen, Aperture, Connection, Url, shape, shard } from "@vivalence/typology";

const aperture = new Aperture();
aperture.open("/echo", (input, ctx) => ({ echoed: input }));
aperture.open("/walled", (input, ctx) => {
  ctx.response.status = 401;
  return { status: "ERROR", error: { code: "MISSING_TOKEN" } };
});
let released = 0;
aperture.open("/ticks", (input, ctx) => {
  ctx.response.body = (async function* () {
    try {
      for (let tick = 0; tick < 3; tick++) {
        yield { tick };
        await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 5));
      }
    } finally {
      released++;
    }
  })();
});

const connect = () => new Connection(new Url("http://inline"), shard.transmitter.inline(shape.http(aperture)));

specimen.describe("shard.transmitter.inline — a Connection over an in-process fetch handler", () => {
  specimen.it("call: a JSON body goes in and comes back, no socket", async () => {
    specimen.expect(await connect().call("/echo", { hello: "ghost" })).toEqual({ echoed: { hello: "ghost" } });
  });

  specimen.it("401 surfaces as the connection's error, the body kept", async () => {
    const response = await connect().fetch("/walled", {});
    specimen.expect(response.status).toBe(401);
    specimen.expect(response.isAuthError).toBe(true);
    specimen.expect(response.body.error.code).toBe("MISSING_TOKEN");
  });

  specimen.it("stream: an async generator leaf arrives as SSE frames", async () => {
    const ticks = [];
    for await (const frame of connect().stream("/ticks")) ticks.push(frame.tick);
    specimen.expect(ticks).toEqual([0, 1, 2]);
  });

  specimen.it("a reader that stops ends the server's generator", async () => {
    const before = released;
    const stream = connect().stream("/ticks");
    const first = await stream.next();
    specimen.expect(first.value.tick).toBe(0);
    await stream.return();
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 20));
    specimen.expect(released).toBe(before + 1);
  });
});
#+END_SRC

The =/echo= effect takes two arguments on purpose: an effect of declared arity 1 receives the CONTEXT (=steer/strategy.js:3-7=, =codemap/typology.md=) — the first draft's =(input) => ({ echoed: input })= echoed the whole context. Run: =deno test -A --no-check subsystems/typology/tests/gestalten/shard/transmitter.inline.test.js=. The last case is the one that reds on today's tree (no =cancel=: the generator never reaches its =finally=) and greens on the =Response.stream= change — reasoned at the fold, not yet run.
** M8 — the runtime host
beef's two modalities, his words — /"we might run runtime inline, or run it embedded where we launch the runtime as a separate process that survives its child"/ — plus the case every client meets first: the instance is already ALIVE and is joined. =host(paladin, ctx)= answers one of three and always with the same face: =launch=, =connect(url)= (a Connection to any address under the runtime, for the lighthouse), =reach(url, lighthouse)= (the dossier's transport for a daemon, M6's seam), =disintegrate()=. Inline is =run.js='s die, built the same way (=run.js:9-13=), executed on the same vector, awaited the same way (=control.controlled=, =R/entities/Process.ts:31=), torn down with the same =kill("SIGTERM")= (=run.js:17=) — with the lock written as =Die= writes it (=paladin/prototypes/ledger/die.js:25-35=) so =instance/stop= and =instances/list= read a hosting ghost as they read a supervisor. Embedded is =instance/start runtime=, called through =ctx.call=, then joined.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/belt/host.js :mkdirp yes
import { lifecycle } from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { Connection, Controller, Span, Url, control, shard, shape, steer } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { remote } from "../client/reach.js";
import { register } from "../trajectories/instance/target.js";

const SIGNALS = ["SIGINT", "SIGTERM", "SIGQUIT"];
const SETTLED = ["DONE", "STOPPED", "FAILED", "ABORTED"];
const PROBE = 2_000;

export async function host(paladin, ctx, { start = true } = {}) {
  await lifecycle.mount(paladin.instance);
  const slug = await register();
  const lock = paladin.ledger.lock(slug);
  const held = await lock.read();
  if (held?.status === "ALIVE") return joined(held);
  if (held) throw new Error(`${slug} is ${String(held.status).toLowerCase()} under pid ${held.pid} — wait for it, or viva instance/stop`);
  if (await answering(paladin.instance.runtime.statics)) return joined(null);
  if ((paladin.instance.runtime.statics.launch ?? "embedded") === "embedded") {
    if (!start) throw new Error(`${slug} is not running — viva instance/start`);
    const started = await ctx.call(["instance/start", "runtime"]);
    if (started?.error) throw new Error(started.error);
    return joined(await lock.read());
  }
  return inline(paladin, ctx, lock, slug);
}

const answering = ({ remote, serve }) =>
  new Connection(remote ?? serve)
    .use(shard.connection.timeout(PROBE))
    .call("/status")
    .then((status) => status?.code === "RUNNING", () => false);

function joined(held) {
  return {
    launch: "joined",
    held,
    connect: (url) => new Connection(new Url(url), shard.transmitter.retry(shard.transmitter.fetcher, { maxRetries: 2 })),
    reach: remote,
    async disintegrate() {},
  };
}

async function inline(paladin, ctx, lock, slug) {
  const { Runtime, lifecycle: runtime } = await import("@vivalence/runtime");
  const token = crypto.randomUUID();
  const started = new Date().toISOString();
  const record = (status) => ({ pid: Deno.pid, token, instance: slug, status, processes: [{ process: "ghost", pid: Deno.pid }], started });
  const release = async () => {
    if ((await lock.read())?.token === token) await lock.remove();
  };
  await lock.write(record("BOOTING"));

  const die = {
    controller: new Controller({ stdout: new Span(`ghost/${paladin.instance.runtime.manifest.slug}`) }),
    mask: paladin.instance.runtime,
    runtime: new Runtime({ instance: paladin.instance }),
  };
  if (ctx.span) die.controller.stdout.to((held) => ctx.span.mark(held.verb, { path: held.path, ...held.data }));
  const stop = () => die.controller.kill("SIGTERM", "signal");
  for (const signal of SIGNALS) Deno.addSignalListener(signal, stop);
  const unlisten = () => SIGNALS.forEach((signal) => Deno.removeSignalListener(signal, stop));

  const running = steer.dispatch.execute(runtime.runtime.execution, die);
  try {
    await control.controlled(die.controller);
  } catch (error) {
    unlisten();
    await running.catch(() => null);
    await release();
    throw error;
  }
  await lock.write(record("ALIVE"));

  const transport = shard.transmitter.inline(shape.http(die.runtime.aperture));
  return {
    launch: "inline",
    die,
    connect: (url) => new Connection(new Url(url), transport),
    reach: (url, lighthouse) => ({
      connection: new Connection(new Url(url), transport)
        .use(shard.connection.authorize(lighthouse.$authority))
        .use(shard.track.span((call) => call.request.url.pathname, lighthouse.channel))
        .use(shard.track.request())
        .use(shard.track.fault()),
    }),
    async disintegrate() {
      unlisten();
      if (!die.controller.status.is(SETTLED)) die.controller.kill("SIGTERM", "ghost: session over");
      await running;
      await release();
    },
  };
}
#+END_SRC

What binds here, hazard by hazard (terrain): the ghost IS the paladin singleton's one instance (4–8) — =register()= reads =paladin.instance.home=; the runtime's =registry.supply()= resets the pensieve ghost may have read for a lens (14) — the lens is re-read on its next use; module-level Vectors are mutated per boot (15) — one boot per process: a held row that is not ALIVE (a supervisor or another ghost BOOTING) is REFUSED by name, never overwritten — the first draft wrote its own BOOTING row over it, two runtimes on one sqlite (17); the lock is still read-then-written, the same window =Die.resolve= has (=die.js:43-51=), named under =* deferred=; the port is =VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE= (16) — bound by the inline =Deno.serve= (=integration.js:25=) so anima reaches a hosted runtime too; the daemons' beacons go over HTTP to that port (21), as they do today. Embedded starts =instance/start runtime= — the bare verb starts =specs("all")= (=target.js:37-58=), anima's vite with it, detached; and a caller that must not leave an instance running behind it (the login, M9) passes ={ start: false }= and is told to start it. An inner failure of the start comes back as ={ error }= (M1's =ctx.call=) and is thrown here — the first draft joined a =null= lock. =@vivalence/runtime= is imported INSIDE =inline()=: =lighthouse.js= imports the host at module scope, and every =viva= — =viva /help= included (=world/map.md=) — would otherwise load the runtime barrel (MikroORM, every lifecycle). =control.controlled= rejects with =status.error ?? new Error(code)= on FAILED (=belt/control.js:3=): a recipe that does not boot throws out of =host= with the runtime's own fault, the lock cleared. The bearer in inline mode rides the =Authorization= header (=shard.connection.authorize=), which the daemon's =identify= reads (=R/prototypes/lighthouse.js:7=) — the frame token is the multiplex's way, not the law. An inline runtime's own console taps (=entities/Activity.ts:18= =[hal …]=, the multiplex upgrade line, =[probe] retry=) print into the ghost's terminal; ink patches the console above its frame — noise, not breakage, named under =* deferred=.

The tests boot the localhost recipe in-process on a scratch ledger, the way =runtime/tests/runtime/boot.test.js= boots chess in a child. The scratch lives in a rig, not in a test file — a test that imports another test file registers and runs that file's tests too:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/rig.js :mkdirp yes
import paladin, { lifecycle } from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { join } from "@std/path";
import { envfile } from "../belt/index.js";

const RECIPE = new URL("../../../commons/instances/localhost/instance.viva.js", import.meta.url).pathname;

export const IN_PROCESS = { sanitizeOps: false, sanitizeResources: false };

function free() {
  const listener = Deno.listen({ port: 0 });
  const { port } = listener.addr;
  listener.close();
  return port;
}

export async function scratch() {
  const ledger = await Deno.makeTempDir({ prefix: "ghost-host-ledger-" });
  const mount = join(ledger, "instances", "localhost");
  await Deno.mkdir(mount, { recursive: true });
  await Deno.copyFile(RECIPE, join(mount, "instance.viva.js"));
  const origin = `http://127.0.0.1:${free()}`;
  paladin.env.set("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT", ledger, "flag");
  paladin.env.set("VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT", mount, "flag");
  await paladin.ledger.instances.write("localhost", { mount });
  await lifecycle.mount(lifecycle.populate.instance(paladin));
  const env = paladin.instance.home.branch(".env");
  await paladin.state.text(env, envfile.scaffold(paladin.instance.environment));
  await paladin.state.env(env, { VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN: origin });
  await lifecycle.mount(lifecycle.populate.instance(paladin));
  const restore = async () => {
    paladin.env.delete("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT");
    paladin.env.delete("VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT");
    await Deno.remove(ledger, { recursive: true });
  };
  return { ledger, mount, origin, restore };
}
#+END_SRC

=127.0.0.1=, not =localhost=: =Deno.serve= binds =[::1]= for =localhost= and =fetch= picks =127.0.0.1= (=codemap/runtime.md=, =project_localhost_ipv6_serve_split=). The sanitizers are opted out by name: an in-process boot leaves the shutdown =STOPPED= beacon in flight after the server closed (known issue =stopped-beacon-lands-after-the-server-closes=) and, for the client, its subscriptions open — a child-process boot never showed the runner either.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/host.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assert, assertEquals, assertRejects } from "@std/assert";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { host } from "../belt/host.js";
import { IN_PROCESS, scratch } from "./rig.js";

Deno.test({
  name: "host: launch inline boots the recipe in this process, writes the lock as ghost, joins itself, releases",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    const { origin, restore } = await scratch();
    const calls = [];
    const ctx = { call: async (argv) => calls.push(argv), span: null };
    const hosting = await host(paladin, ctx);
    try {
      assertEquals(hosting.launch, "inline");
      assertEquals(calls, []);
      const lock = await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read();
      assertEquals(lock.status, "ALIVE");
      assertEquals(lock.pid, Deno.pid);
      assertEquals(lock.processes, [{ process: "ghost", pid: Deno.pid }]);
      assertEquals((await hosting.connect(origin).call("/status")).code, "RUNNING");
      const again = await host(paladin, ctx);
      assertEquals(again.launch, "joined");
      assertEquals(again.held.token, lock.token);
      const over = await fetch(`${origin}/status`).then((response) => response.json());
      assertEquals(over.code, "RUNNING");
    } finally {
      await hosting.disintegrate();
    }
    assertEquals(await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read(), null);
    assert(hosting.die.controller.status.is("STOPPED"));
    await restore();
  },
});

Deno.test({
  name: "host: a RUNNING runtime that holds no lock — a watcher's — is joined, never booted twice",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    const { restore } = await scratch();
    const hosting = await host(paladin, { call: async () => ({}), span: null });
    try {
      await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").remove();
      const again = await host(paladin, { call: async () => ({ error: "no second start" }), span: null });
      assertEquals(again.launch, "joined");
      assertEquals(again.held, null);
      assertEquals(await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read(), null);
    } finally {
      await hosting.disintegrate();
      await restore();
    }
  },
});

Deno.test("host: a held lock that is not ALIVE is refused, never overwritten", async () => {
  const { restore } = await scratch();
  const held = { pid: Deno.pid, token: "theirs", instance: "localhost", status: "BOOTING", processes: [{ process: "runtime", pid: Deno.pid }], started: new Date().toISOString() };
  await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").write(held);
  try {
    await assertRejects(() => host(paladin, { call: async () => ({}) }), Error, "booting under pid");
    assertEquals((await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read()).token, "theirs");
  } finally {
    await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").remove();
    await restore();
  }
});
#+END_SRC

=Lock.read= pings the pid with =SIGURG= and clears a dead lock (=lock.js:9-18=), so the refusal case writes its row under the test's own live pid. Run: =deno test -A --no-check systems/ghost/tests/host.test.js= — one process, no child; =pgrep -f runtime/run= stays empty for its duration (P-host-no-child). The embedded road is walked, not unit-tested: =instance/start= is covered by its own walk and the join is the ALIVE branch this test exercises.
** M9 — credentials
beef: /"we add a new env var viva-credentials-file where we either default to vivay or swt linuxy if wanted. both possible. we can store many credentials! many lighthouses."/ · /"home is an identity trait."/ One file, one row per lighthouse origin — anima's =lighthouse:<url>= law on disk, mode 0600, at =VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE= else =<ledger>/credentials.json=. The key is declared where paladin declares its other paths (F2 as tangled: a =FILES= environment beside =MOUNTS=, because a FILE is one file and =belt/config.js:6= shears =_MOUNT= to make a flag); the store is a =Ledger= organ like =instances= and =locks=; the schematic carries the row's shape with the auth primitives that exist (=schematics/primitives/auth.js= =AuthorityToken=). The identity card the lighthouse answers is ={ id, slug, traits, trait }= (=L/attachment/auth.js:15=) — =trait.HOMED= is where =home= lives, read by =credentials.home(origin)=; no route writes identity traits today (grep 10-05: =HOMED= has zero writers, =THEMED= one reader), so the row's =trait= is what signup/login returned — the writer is deferred.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/credentials.js :mkdirp yes
import { dirname } from "@std/path";
import { Path, v } from "@vivalence/typology";

const KEY = "VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE";
const MODE = 0o600;

const origin = (address) => (address?.absolute ?? String(address)).replace(/\/$/, "");

export class Credentials {
  constructor(paladin) {
    this.paladin = paladin;
  }

  get path() {
    const declared = this.paladin.env.get(KEY);
    return declared ? new Path(declared) : this.paladin.scope.ledger.branch("credentials.json");
  }

  read() {
    return this.paladin.read.json(this.path, {});
  }

  async lookup(address) {
    return (await this.read())[origin(address)] ?? null;
  }

  async write(address, credential) {
    const all = await this.read();
    all[origin(address)] = { ...credential, at: new Date().toISOString() };
    const faults = v.primitives.credentials.Credentials.faults(all);
    if (faults.length) throw new Error(`credentials: ${faults.map((fault) => `${fault.at} ${fault.reason}`).join(" · ")}`);
    await this.keep(all);
  }

  async remove(address) {
    const all = await this.read();
    delete all[origin(address)];
    await this.keep(all);
  }

  async home(address) {
    return (await this.lookup(address))?.identity?.trait?.HOMED?.mode ?? null;
  }

  store(address) {
    return {
      read: async () => {
        const held = await this.lookup(address);
        return held ? { authority: held.authority, identity: held.identity } : null;
      },
      write: ({ authority, identity }) =>
        authority?.access && authority?.refresh && identity?.id ? this.write(address, { authority, identity }) : undefined,
      remove: () => this.remove(address),
    };
  }

  async keep(all) {
    const file = this.path.absolute;
    await this.paladin.state.dir(dirname(file));
    await Deno.writeTextFile(file, JSON.stringify(all, null, 2), { mode: MODE });
    await Deno.chmod(file, MODE);
  }
}
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/typology/schematics/primitives/credentials.js :mkdirp yes
import { v } from "../v.js";
import { ID } from "../scalars/index.js";
import { AuthorityToken } from "./auth.js";

export const Card = v.object(
  {
    id: ID,
    slug: v.string({ minLength: 1 }).desc("the identity's slug — a multiplayer signup stores the USERNAME here, unvalidated. Example: beef"),
    traits: v.array(v.string()).default([]),
    trait: v.object({}, { additionalProperties: true }).default({}),
  },
  { additionalProperties: true, description: "the identity as a lighthouse answers it at signup, login and verify" },
);

export const Credential = v.object({
  authority: AuthorityToken,
  identity: Card,
  at: v.string().desc("ISO time of the last write. Example: 2026-10-05T09:12:33.000Z"),
});

export const Credentials = v.record(v.string(), Credential).desc("one row per lighthouse origin, as consumed and without a trailing slash");
#+END_SRC

The store answers a write only for a WHOLE row — =login= sets =authority= before =identity=, =logout= nulls them one at a time, and each half-step reaches the effect (M6); a half row is skipped, the next step completes or removes it. The file is born =0600= (=writeTextFile= with =mode= applies at creation; the =chmod= after covers a file that predates it) — the first draft wrote it =0644= and chmodded after, tokens world-readable in between. =Card.slug= is a plain non-empty string, not =Slug=: =slug()= is kebab-only (=scalars/index.js:7=) and multiplayer's =/signup= stores the username unvalidated (=attachment/auth.js:31=) — =Finn= or =beef_01= would log in and then fail to persist. =JWTToken= (=scalars/index.js:10=, three base64url segments) passes the tests' =a.b.c= rows — checked at the fold.

=HOMED= as a trait object ={ mode: "@owner/type/slug" }= follows the lighthouse's trait convention (=terminal.trait.DOCKED= · =thread.trait.INTELLIGENT= · =identity.trait.THEMED.theme=, =+page.svelte:62=): the key is the trait's name, the value its config. Ghost: the flag, the verb, the doctor.

The verb, whole — 14 lines today (=trajectories/instance/lighthouse.js=, quoted in the terrain), reaching the lighthouse through =host()= so it works on a cold inline instance as on a running one:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/lighthouse.js
import paladin, { lifecycle } from "@vivalence/paladin";
import * as jose from "jose";
import { host } from "../../belt/host.js";

const ACTIONS = ["signup", "login", "password", "logout"];

const operator = (secret) =>
  new jose.SignJWT({ type: "operator" })
    .setProtectedHeader({ alg: "HS256" })
    .setIssuedAt()
    .setExpirationTime("2m")
    .sign(new TextEncoder().encode(secret));

export async function lighthouse(ctx) {
  const [action, username, password] = ctx.signal.params;
  if (!ACTIONS.includes(action) || (action !== "logout" && (!username || !password))) {
    return (ctx.effect = { error: "usage: /instance/lighthouse <signup|login|password> <username> <password> | logout" });
  }
  await lifecycle.mount(paladin.instance);
  const origin = paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote;
  const address = origin?.absolute ?? String(origin);
  const credentials = paladin.ledger.credentials;
  const hosting = await host(paladin, ctx, { start: false });
  try {
    const connection = hosting.connect(origin);
    if (action === "logout") {
      const held = await credentials.lookup(origin);
      if (held) await connection.call("/auth/logout", { refresh: held.authority.refresh }).catch(() => null);
      await credentials.remove(origin);
      return (ctx.effect = { status: "LOGGED_OUT", origin: address, stored: credentials.path.absolute });
    }
    const proof = action === "password" ? { operator: await operator(paladin.secret.get("SECRET_VIVA_JWT")) } : {};
    const answer = await connection.call(`/auth/${action}`, { ...proof, username, password });
    if (answer.authority) await credentials.write(origin, { authority: answer.authority, identity: answer.identity });
    ctx.effect = { ...answer, stored: credentials.path.absolute };
  } finally {
    await hosting.disintegrate();
  }
}
#+END_SRC

A login that hosts an inline runtime for the call and tears it down after is the price of /"logged in … runtime started if instance not already live"/ in that order; on a joined instance =disintegrate= is a no-op; on a stopped =embedded= instance the login does NOT start one (={ start: false }=) — it says =viva instance/start=, as the verb answers today when nothing listens. =statics.remote= decodes to a typology =Url= (=schematics/prototypes/signatures.js:28= =Decode((held) => new Prototype(held))=), so the effect prints its =absolute=, never =String(url)= (="[object Object]"=).

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../subsystems/paladin/tests/credentials.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assertEquals, assertRejects } from "@std/assert";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";

const row = (slug) => ({ authority: { access: "a.b.c", refresh: "d.e.f" }, identity: { id: `${slug}-id`, slug, traits: [], trait: {} } });

Deno.test("credentials: write · lookup · remove · home, under the ledger by default, mode 0600", async () => {
  const ledger = await Deno.makeTempDir({ prefix: "credentials-" });
  paladin.env.set("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT", ledger, "flag");
  paladin.env.delete("VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE");
  const credentials = paladin.ledger.credentials;
  assertEquals(credentials.path.absolute, `${ledger}/credentials.json`);
  await credentials.write("http://a:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/x/", row("alpha"));
  await credentials.write("http://b:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/y", { ...row("beta"), identity: { ...row("beta").identity, traits: ["HOMED"], trait: { HOMED: { mode: "@education/home/aprende" } } } });
  assertEquals((await credentials.lookup("http://a:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/x")).identity.slug, "alpha");
  assertEquals(await credentials.home("http://b:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/y"), "@education/home/aprende");
  assertEquals(await credentials.home("http://a:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/x"), null);
  assertEquals((await Deno.stat(credentials.path.absolute)).mode & 0o777, 0o600);
  await credentials.remove("http://a:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/x");
  assertEquals(await credentials.lookup("http://a:2501/attached/process/service/lighthouse/x"), null);
  assertEquals(Object.keys(await credentials.read()).length, 1);
  paladin.env.delete("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT");
});

Deno.test("credentials: the env key moves the file; a malformed row is refused", async () => {
  const dir = await Deno.makeTempDir({ prefix: "credentials-linuxy-" });
  paladin.env.set("VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE", `${dir}/credentials.json`, "flag");
  const credentials = paladin.ledger.credentials;
  assertEquals(credentials.path.absolute, `${dir}/credentials.json`);
  await credentials.write("http://c:2501", row("gamma"));
  assertEquals(JSON.parse(await Deno.readTextFile(`${dir}/credentials.json`))["http://c:2501"].identity.slug, "gamma");
  await assertRejects(() => credentials.write("http://d:2501", { authority: { access: "only" }, identity: {} }));
  paladin.env.delete("VIVA_CLIENT_GHOST_CREDENTIALS_FILE");
});

Deno.test("credentials: the store a lighthouse hydrates from answers the three verbs", async () => {
  const ledger = await Deno.makeTempDir({ prefix: "credentials-store-" });
  paladin.env.set("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT", ledger, "flag");
  const store = paladin.ledger.credentials.store("http://e:2501");
  assertEquals(await store.read(), null);
  await store.write(row("epsilon"));
  assertEquals((await store.read()).identity.slug, "epsilon");
  await store.remove();
  assertEquals(await store.read(), null);
  paladin.env.delete("VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT");
});
#+END_SRC

The AuthorityToken scalar =JWTToken= (=schematics/scalars=) decides whether =a.b.c= passes as a token; if it demands a real JWT shape the fixture rows carry three base64url segments — the test is adjusted at land, never the scalar.
** M10 — the ghost client
anima's =+layout.svelte:28-70= without Svelte: one =Lighthouse= over the host's connection, hydrated from the ledger's row, booted (=verify → refresh → populate=, =lighthouse.js:232-237=), every daemon awaited to =healthy= — the dossier's =mounting= (un-awaited in the browser, =dossier.js:72=) is what handshakes each daemon and seats the user row (=/userspace/handshake=, =R/lifecycle/daemon/aperture/userspace.js:6=), so awaiting it IS /"terminals and daemons loaded"/. The roster is bearer-walled since #251 (terrain gap 1), so every announced daemon is listed and every listed daemon is mounted.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/belt/client.js :mkdirp yes
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { Lighthouse, Terminals, boot, hydrate, logger } from "../client/mod.js";

const MOUNTING = 30_000;

export async function client(hosting, { channel = logger.channel } = {}) {
  const origin = paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote;
  const address = origin?.absolute ?? String(origin);
  const lighthouse = new Lighthouse(hosting.connect(origin), { channel, reach: hosting.reach });
  await hydrate(lighthouse, paladin.ledger.credentials.store(origin));
  const authorized = await boot(lighthouse).catch(() => false);
  if (!authorized) throw new Error(`ghost: not logged in at ${address} — viva instance/lighthouse login <username> <password>`);

  const daemons = lighthouse.$daemons.get();
  let timer;
  const deadline = new Promise((resolve) => (timer = setTimeout(resolve, MOUNTING, "late")));
  let outcomes;
  try {
    outcomes = await Promise.all(daemons.map((daemon) => Promise.race([daemon.mounting, deadline])));
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }
  const refused = daemons.filter((daemon, at) => outcomes[at] !== "mounted");
  if (refused.length) {
    throw new Error(`ghost: ${refused.map((daemon) => `${daemon.slug} ${daemon.status.reflection.code}`).join(", ")} — viva instance/doctor`);
  }

  return { origin: address, lighthouse, daemons, terminals: new Terminals(lighthouse.dataspace.terminal) };
}
#+END_SRC

=boot= returns =false= when unauthorized and =verifyAuth= THROWS "Lighthouse unauthorized" on a verified-but-tokenless lighthouse (=lighthouse.js:224-230=) — both read as "not logged in". =mounting= resolves ="mounted" · "error"= and retries forever on ="unreachable"= (=dossier.js:73-78=); the deadline turns a daemon that never comes into a named refusal instead of a hang, and is cleared either way. =Terminals= wraps the lighthouse's terminal repository (=stores/terminals.js:7=); =create()= seats the identity server-side (=T/entities/lighthouse/Terminal.ts:12=). Three things the first draft had wrong: the =channel= defaulted to =null=, and every daemon request opens =shard.track.span(…, channel)= (=reach.js=) — =new Span(nature).to(null)= → =Pipe.to= → =fromm.sink(null).write= THROWS (=gestalten/fromm/index.js:17=) on the first call, so the client's own =logger.channel= is the default (anima passes its own, the same pipe); the =origin= it handed the dock was the decoded =Url= object, which ink cannot render as a child — the client answers the address string; the deadline timer outlived the race by 30 s.

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/client.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assert, assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { host } from "../belt/host.js";
import { client } from "../belt/client.js";
import { IN_PROCESS, scratch } from "./rig.js";

Deno.test({
  name: "client: on an inline localhost — login persists, boot lists and mounts every daemon, a terminal seats and goes",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    const { restore } = await scratch();
    const hosting = await host(paladin, { call: async () => ({}), span: null });
    try {
      const lighthouse = paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote;
      const answer = await hosting.connect(lighthouse).call("/auth/login", { username: "anyone", password: "anything" });
      await paladin.ledger.credentials.write(lighthouse, { authority: answer.authority, identity: answer.identity });

      const held = await client(hosting);
      assertEquals(typeof held.origin, "string");
      assertEquals(held.lighthouse.identity.slug, "localhost");
      assertEquals(held.daemons.map((daemon) => daemon.slug), ["hello"]);
      assert(held.daemons.every((daemon) => daemon.status.is("healthy")));
      const hello = held.daemons[0];
      assert(hello.modes.chaosmonkey?.oracle, "the oracle is reachable through its daemon");
      assert(typeof hello.modes.chaosmonkey.oracle.harness?.dialogue?.stream === "function", "HARNESSED wired over the inline transport");

      const terminal = await held.terminals.create();
      assertEquals(terminal.identity.id ?? terminal.identity, held.lighthouse.identity.id);
      const thread = await hello.entities.thread.create({ mode: hello.modes.chaosmonkey.oracle.id });
      await terminal.seat(thread);
      assertEquals((await held.lighthouse.connection.call("/entities/terminal/findOne", { where: { id: terminal.id } })).thread, thread.id);
      await held.terminals.remove(terminal.id);
      assertEquals(await held.lighthouse.connection.call("/entities/terminal/findOne", { where: { id: terminal.id } }), null);
    } finally {
      await hosting.disintegrate();
      await restore();
    }
  },
});

Deno.test({
  name: "client: without a row the boot refuses with the login hint",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    const { restore } = await scratch();
    const hosting = await host(paladin, { call: async () => ({}), span: null });
    try {
      let refused = null;
      try {
        await client(hosting);
      } catch (error) {
        refused = error;
      }
      assert(refused?.message.includes("viva instance/lighthouse login"));
    } finally {
      await hosting.disintegrate();
      await restore();
    }
  },
});
#+END_SRC

The scratch comes from =tests/rig.js= (M8) — one scratch ledger per test, torn down by =restore()=; a fresh scratch holds no row, so the refusal case needs no removal. Run: =deno test -A --no-check systems/ghost/tests/client.test.js= — this is the marker P-enroll-roster: a fresh identity's handshake on every daemon, the mode route answering without =USER_NOT_FOUND=. Each test boots the runtime once; a run of both files boots it three times in one process — the lifecycle Vectors are per-boot objects and each scratch its own sqlite and port, but hazard 15 (module-level Vectors touched per boot) is exactly what a multi-boot run would surface: run the files one at a time until the critical pass says otherwise.
** M11 — the session, captured
beef: /"the trajectory a possible terminal spawner … might make sense to re-mount the trajectory inside the interactive cli"/. The session is the shell's head: =view.capture({ session, face }, { captured: Captured, chrome: Crowned })= FIRST, then the gate under it — host → client → one terminal row → seat → harness, five step rows the faces paint (=faces/Boot.jsx=, M12; pass 2: the first draft captured after the client, so the gate had no screen); every line the Dock reads goes through =session.more(line)= = =ctx.call(Signal.tokens(line), { session, shell })= — the SAME trajectory, the same middlewares, the argv grammar — so a command paints into the slot (the render middleware carries the capture, M3), a reference reseats (=reference= reads =ctx.session=, M4), a sentence is a turn (=yap= reads =ctx.session=, M4). anima's =d.svelte:83-112 selectMode= is =seat=; anima's =Dock.svelte:157-184 send()= is =turn=. Esc tears down in reverse: the row (=Terminals.remove=, =stores/terminals.js:32=), the host (=disintegrate=).

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/belt/session.js :mkdirp yes
import { atom } from "nanostores";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { Signal, soma } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { view } from "@vivalence/sheets";
import { host } from "./host.js";
import { client } from "./client.js";
import { boot } from "../client/mod.js";
import { until } from "../trajectories/instance/target.js";
import { Captured } from "../trajectories/faces/Captured.jsx";
import { Crowned } from "../trajectories/faces/Crowned.jsx";
import { SignIn } from "../trajectories/faces/Boot.jsx";
import { Left } from "../trajectories/faces/Turn.jsx";

export async function open(ctx, row, first = "") {
  const session = new Session(ctx);
  const face = ctx.signal?.flags?.chrome ? "chrome" : "captured";
  const capture = ctx.view.capture({ session, face }, { captured: Captured, chrome: Crowned });
  session.capture = capture;
  try {
    await session.boot(row, first, ctx.then);
  } catch (error) {
    capture.controller.kill("SIGKILL", error.message);
    await capture.settled;
    await session.close();
    throw error;
  }
  const outcome = await capture.settled;
  const summary = session.summary();
  await session.close();
  await view.cut.print(summary, Left);
  return { ...outcome, ...summary, session: session.toJSON() };
}

const STEPS = ["instance", "lighthouse", "daemons", "terminal", "harness"];

export class Session {
  $boot = atom(STEPS.map((label) => ({ label, status: "pending", detail: "" })));
  $tail = atom([]);
  $standing = atom("connecting");
  $terminal = atom(null);
  $step = atom("thread");
  $live = atom(null);
  $echo = atom(null);
  $error = atom(null);
  $notes = atom([]);
  capture = null;
  hosting = null;
  held = null;
  seated = null;
  made = new Set();

  constructor(ctx) {
    Object.assign(this, { ctx, instance: paladin.instance.manifest.slug });
  }

  get terminal() {
    return this.$terminal.get();
  }

  get thread() {
    return this.terminal?.$thread.get() ?? null;
  }

  step(label, status, detail = "") {
    this.$boot.set(this.$boot.get().map((step) => (step.label === label ? { label, status, detail } : step)));
  }

  note(text) {
    this.$notes.set([...this.$notes.get(), { id: crypto.randomUUID(), note: text, createdAt: new Date().toISOString() }]);
  }

  async boot(row, first, then) {
    const slug = this.instance;
    this.step("instance", "running", `${slug} · reading the lock`);
    this.hosting = await host(paladin, this.ctx, { tail: (line) => this.$tail.set([...this.$tail.get().slice(-5), line]) });
    this.$tail.set([]);
    const pid = this.hosting.held?.pid;
    this.step("instance", "done", this.hosting.launch === "joined" ? `${slug} · lock ALIVE${pid ? ` pid ${pid}` : ""} · joined` : `${slug} · inline · pid ${Deno.pid} · lock taken`);
    const origin = String(paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote?.absolute ?? paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote);
    this.step("lighthouse", "running", `${origin} · checking`);
    this.step("daemons", "running", "mounting");
    this.held = await client(this.hosting, { channel: this.ctx.span?.pipe, login: (lighthouse) => this.signin(lighthouse, origin) });
    this.$standing.set("verified");
    this.step("lighthouse", "done", `${origin} · verified · ${this.held.lighthouse.identity?.slug ?? "—"}`);
    const modes = this.rows().length;
    this.step("daemons", "done", `${this.held.daemons.map((daemon) => `● ${daemon.slug}`).join("  ")} · ${modes} mode${modes === 1 ? "" : "s"}`);
    this.step("terminal", "running", "creating a row on the lighthouse");
    const terminal = await this.held.terminals.create();
    this.made.add(terminal.id);
    this.$terminal.set(terminal);
    await this.seat(row);
    this.step("terminal", "done", `seated on a new thread of ${row.daemon} › ${row.type}/${row.slug}`);
    const harness = this.thread?.mode?.harness;
    this.step("harness", harness?.dialogue ? "done" : "warn", harness?.dialogue ? "connected · dialogue/stream" : "no dialogue harness — a turn has nowhere to go");
    this.$boot.set(null);
    if (first) await this.turn(first);
    if (then) await this.more(then);
  }

  async signin(lighthouse, origin) {
    this.$standing.set("signed out");
    this.step("lighthouse", "warn", `${origin} · signed out`);
    let error = null;
    while (true) {
      const typed = await this.capture.cut.hold({ origin, error }, SignIn);
      if (typed.aborted) return false;
      this.$standing.set("checking");
      this.step("lighthouse", "running", `${origin} · /auth/login ${typed.username}`);
      try {
        await lighthouse.login(typed.username, typed.password);
        if (await boot(lighthouse)) return true;
        error = "the lighthouse refused the token it just signed";
      } catch (caught) {
        error = caught.message;
      }
      this.$standing.set("signed out");
      this.step("lighthouse", "warn", `${origin} · ${error}`);
    }
  }

  rows() {
    return this.held.daemons.flatMap((daemon) =>
      Object.entries(daemon.modes).flatMap(([type, slugs]) => Object.keys(slugs).map((slug) => ({ daemon: daemon.slug, type, slug })))
    );
  }

  async seat(row, first = "") {
    const daemon = this.held.daemons.find((held) => held.slug === row.daemon);
    const mode = daemon?.modes[row.type]?.[row.slug];
    if (!mode) throw new Error(`${row.daemon}/${row.type}/${row.slug}: not mounted — viva instance/doctor`);
    const again = Boolean(this.thread);
    const thread = await daemon.entities.thread.create({ mode: mode.id });
    await this.terminal.seat(thread);
    if (!(await until(() => this.thread?.id === thread.id, 5_000))) throw new Error(`${row.slug}: the terminal did not take the thread`);
    this.seated = row;
    this.$step.set("thread");
    this.note(`── ${again ? "reseated" : "seated"} · ${row.daemon} › ${row.type}/${row.slug} · new thread`);
    if (first) await this.turn(first);
    return { seated: `${row.daemon}/${row.type}/${row.slug}`, thread: thread.id };
  }

  activate(terminal) {
    this.held.terminals.activate(terminal.id);
    this.$terminal.set(terminal);
    const thread = terminal.$thread.get();
    this.seated = thread?.mode ? { daemon: thread.daemon.slug, type: thread.mode.type, slug: thread.mode.slug } : null;
    this.$step.set(this.seated ? "thread" : "mode");
    return { active: terminal.id, seated: this.seated };
  }

  async open() {
    const terminal = await this.held.terminals.create();
    this.made.add(terminal.id);
    return this.activate(terminal);
  }

  async drop(terminal) {
    if (this.held.terminals.entities.length === 1) return { error: "the last terminal stays — esc leaves the session" };
    const turns = terminal.$thread.get()?.$turns.get().length ?? 0;
    await this.held.terminals.remove(terminal.id);
    this.made.delete(terminal.id);
    if (terminal === this.terminal) this.activate(this.held.terminals.active);
    this.note(`── closed · ${terminal.trait?.LABELED ?? terminal.$thread.get()?.mode?.slug ?? "new"} · ${turns} turn${turns === 1 ? "" : "s"} kept on the lighthouse`);
    return { closed: terminal.id };
  }

  async turn(text) {
    const thread = this.thread;
    if (!thread?.mode?.harness?.dialogue) return { error: "no harnessed thread is seated" };
    if (this.$live.get()) return { error: "a turn is still streaming — ctrl-c stops it" };
    const id = crypto.randomUUID();
    const parts = [{ type: "text", text }];
    this.$echo.set({ id, role: "user", parts, createdAt: new Date().toISOString() });
    this.$error.set(null);
    this.capture?.clear();
    try {
      for await (const turn of soma.scan(thread.mode.harness.dialogue.stream({ thread: thread.id, id, parts }))) this.$live.set({ ...turn });
    } catch (error) {
      this.$error.set(error.message);
    } finally {
      this.$live.set(null);
      this.$echo.set(null);
    }
    return { turned: id };
  }

  async more(line) {
    try {
      const effect = await this.ctx.call(Signal.tokens(line), { session: this, capture: this.capture, rendered: false });
      if (effect?.error) this.$error.set(effect.error);
      return effect;
    } catch (error) {
      this.$error.set(error.message);
      return { error: error.message };
    }
  }

  summary() {
    const turns = this.thread?.$turns.get().length ?? 0;
    return { left: this.seated?.slug ?? "nothing", turns, instance: this.instance, running: this.hosting?.launch === "joined" };
  }

  async close() {
    if (this.held) for (const id of this.made) if (this.held.terminals.entities.some((terminal) => terminal.id === id)) await this.held.terminals.remove(id);
    await this.hosting?.disintegrate();
  }

  toJSON() {
    return { launch: this.hosting?.launch ?? null, origin: this.held?.origin ?? null, identity: this.held?.lighthouse.identity?.slug ?? null, terminal: this.terminal?.id ?? null, seated: this.seated };
  }
}
#+END_SRC

The gate, step by step (=boot=): =instance= is =host()= — joined names the lock's pid, inline shows the runtime's own span as a six-line tail (=host(…, { tail })=, hunk below) until =RUNNING=; =lighthouse= and =daemons= are =client()= — a lighthouse with no credentials row hands the session its =login= callback (hunk below), the standing drops to =signed out=, and =SignIn= holds the slot (=capture.cut.hold=) until a pair of names logs in or esc leaves: =lighthouse.login(username, password)= is the client's own (=client/stores/lighthouse.js:55=, the method anima's =Login.svelte= calls — a function form is M6's call, not this one's), then =boot(lighthouse)= verifies the token it signed; =terminal= creates the row and seats it; =harness= reads the seated mode's =dialogue= — absent, the step warns and the session still opens (a turn then answers =no harnessed thread is seated=). =$boot= set to =null= IS the collapse: the faces paint the transcript from then on. A line carried in as =ctx.then= (a session verb typed from zsh, F21 RULED) runs through =more= once the gate is down, after the first turn if both came. On esc, =open()= prints the one leave line through sheets' own =view.cut.print= (the capture is already unmounted): =✦ left <mode> · <n> turns · <instance> still running | stopped=, and the reference marks the dispatch rendered so =Effect= does not print the summary again (hunk below).

What =seat= waits for: =terminal.seat(thread)= writes the row (=client/entities/terminal.js:51=); =mountSeat.derive= turns the row into =$thread= once the daemon is healthy and the thread sits in its store (=:81-109=) — the =create= merged it there. =until= is =target.js:60=, the same poll =instance/start= uses. =Signal.tokens= is M1's; a typed =instance/doctor group:keys= dispatches as argv, a typed =what did i miss= hits the root remainder, a typed =play e4= hits the reference. The inner dispatch carries the capture AND =rendered: false= — =ctx.call= pre-marks an inner dispatch rendered (a chain prints once, =codemap/ghost.md=), which would leave a typed =instance/doctor= painting nothing; the render middleware (M3) then paints the effect into the Dock's slot, and neither holds nor kills a capture it did not make. =$terminal= is an atom because the session switches terminals (M13): every face reads the active one through it. =made= scopes the teardown: esc removes the terminals THIS session opened, never the identity's older rows on the lighthouse. A failure comes back as ={ error }= (M1's =ctx.call=) or, for a typed line that names no handler under a command noun, as a thrown =NotFound= before any middleware runs (=steer/dispatch.js:63=) — both land on =$error=, the dock's error line. A seat that throws after the capture is painted KILLS the capture before the error leaves: =fail()= exits through =Deno.exit= (=mod.js:147-154=), and an ink root still mounted at that moment leaves the operator's terminal in raw mode.

The =login= callback is the client's one concession to a surface: =client()= stays headless (a =null= callback is today's refusal by name, the M10 test unchanged) and the session is the only caller that passes one. =instance/lighthouse login= (M9) keeps its road for a shell that never opens a session.

M4 tangles =references.js= with its =shell= import already in place; the M4 boundary runs with a stub =belt/session.js=, one file, replaced whole here — the quest records the stub so the boundary is honest:

Teardown order on esc (from captured; in chrome esc steps back to captured first): the dock's =useInput= → =controller.kill("SIGTERM", "esc")= → the capture's =settle= answers with =close= → =capture.settled= resolves ={ aborted: true }= → =open='s =finally= → =session.close()=: the terminal rows this session made removed (anima's roster loses it at once over its subscription), then =hosting.disintegrate()= — a hosted runtime stops and clears its lock, a joined one is left as found. =viva instances/list= from another shell shows =running: ghost= while the session lives and nothing after (T-esc-teardown).

The session needs no terminal to be tested — only its capture does. A fake =view.capture= that hands back a controller and a =settled= the test resolves drives the real =open()= against an inline localhost: the seat, a turn, a typed line, the esc /(pass: added — the first draft left the whole session to the walk)/. On the sandbox's voiceless oracle (no hallucinator key) a turn ends in 15 ms with the server's =500 — [hallucination] no 'dialogue' faculty resolves a 'stream' avenue= on =$error=, never a hang:

#+BEGIN_SRC js :tangle ../../systems/ghost/tests/session.test.js :mkdirp yes
import { assert, assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import paladin from "@vivalence/paladin";
import { Controller, Span } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { host } from "../belt/host.js";
import { open } from "../belt/session.js";
import { until } from "../trajectories/instance/target.js";
import { IN_PROCESS, scratch } from "./rig.js";

const shelled = () => {
  const shells = [];
  const calls = [];
  const ctx = {
    span: null,
    call: async (argv, carried) => (calls.push({ argv, carried: Object.keys(carried).sort() }), { called: argv }),
    view: {
      capture: (data, faces) => {
        let leave;
        const settled = new Promise((resolve) => (leave = resolve));
        const shell = { controller: new Controller({ stdout: new Span("capture") }), settled, data, body: faces.captured.name, face: data.face, shift: () => {}, clear: () => {}, leave, cut: { print: async () => {}, hold: async () => ({ aborted: true }) } };
        shells.push(shell);
        return shell;
      },
    },
  };
  return { ctx, shells, calls };
};

Deno.test({
  name: "session: open seats a thread under a ghost lock, a turn ends, a typed line dispatches as argv, esc tears down",
  ...IN_PROCESS,
  fn: async () => {
    const { restore } = await scratch();
    const login = await host(paladin, { call: async () => ({}), span: null });
    const origin = paladin.instance.lighthouse.statics.remote;
    const answer = await login.connect(origin).call("/auth/login", { username: "anyone", password: "anything" });
    await paladin.ledger.credentials.write(origin, { authority: answer.authority, identity: answer.identity });
    await login.disintegrate();

    const { ctx, shells, calls } = shelled();
    const row = { daemon: "hello", type: "chaosmonkey", slug: "oracle" };
    const opened = open(ctx, row, "");
    const shell = await until(() => shells[0]?.data.session.seated && shells[0], 30_000);
    assert(shell, "the shell painted and the session seated");
    const session = shell.data.session;
    assertEquals(shell.body, "Captured");
    assertEquals(shell.face, "captured");
    assertEquals(session.seated, row);
    assertEquals(session.thread.mode.slug, "oracle");
    assertEquals(session.$boot.get(), null);
    assertEquals(session.$standing.get(), "verified");
    assert(session.$notes.get()[0]?.note.startsWith("── seated · hello › chaosmonkey/oracle"), "the seat leaves a note");
    assertEquals((await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read()).processes, [{ process: "ghost", pid: Deno.pid }]);

    const turned = await Promise.race([session.turn("hello oracle"), new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 15_000, "hung"))]);
    assert(turned?.turned, "a turn ends, whatever the oracle answers");
    assertEquals(session.$live.get(), null);

    await session.more("instance/doctor --json");
    assertEquals(calls, [{ argv: ["instance/doctor", "--json"], carried: ["capture", "rendered", "session"] }]);

    shell.leave({ aborted: true });
    const outcome = await opened;
    assertEquals(outcome.aborted, true);
    assertEquals(outcome.left, "oracle");
    assertEquals(outcome.running, false);
    assertEquals(outcome.session.seated, row);
    assertEquals(outcome.session.launch, "inline");
    assertEquals(await paladin.ledger.lock("localhost").read(), null);
    await restore();
  },
});
#+END_SRC

Ghost exits today by draining its event loop; after a session it never would. The pinhole ends with the one exit, symmetric to =fail()=:
